Grep patterns: \\\"uninterrupted\\\", \\\"sample\\\", \\\"erosion\\\", \\\"episode\\\", \\\"Gini|SWIID|PIP|inequality\\\", \\\"hazard|Cox|logit|OLS|probit\\\", \\\"control\\\", \\\"cluster\\\", \\\"lag\\\", \\\"1995|2000|2020\\\", \\\"threshold\\\".\\nRecord: (a) exact sample definition \u2014 which regime classification screens countries, which countries are excluded (post-1990 democratizers that fell below the democracy threshold?), N countries, N country-years, period; (b) the full erosion-episode list and coding rule (binary onset? sustained V-Dem LDI drop? threshold?); (c) inequality variable: exact source (SWIID/PIP/WID), transformation, lag structure; (d) full control set; (e) model family, fixed effects, clustering; (f) headline coefficients/SEs (needed to calibrate the iter-2 main-effect magnitude); (g) whether they run ANY moderation/interaction analysis (expected: none \u2014 that gap is the gate); (h) mention of replication-data availability. Also spend <=5 min searching for published commentary/replication critiques of the paper.\\n\\nB. Acemoglu, Naidu, Restrepo & Robinson (2015) \u2014 the democratization-dividend design [30 min]\\nLocate NBER w19746 PDF (free) + published-version landing page (ScienceDirect Handbook chapter; verify venue per Phase 0 note). Grep patterns: \\\"democratiz\\\", \\\"event stud\\\", \\\"top decile|top 10|income share\\\", \\\"school|education\\\", \\\"tax\\\", \\\"public good\\\", \\\"matching\\\", \\\"1960\\\", \\\"equation\\\", \\\"cluster\\\", \\\"sample\\\".\\nRecord: (a) democratization-event definition and regime-classification source; (b) sample N countries and years; (c) full outcome list \u2014 top-decile income share (from WID?), tax/GDP share, social/education/health spending, schooling; (d) estimation design: OLS with country+year FE, dynamic leads/lags event-study form, any matching estimator \u2014 write out their actual equation(s) as printed; (e) controls and clustering; (f) headline magnitudes: effect of democratization on top-decile share at +10 years, on public-goods measures (these size the iter-2 welfare-dependent dividend test); (g) their own caveats about the inequality leg (the exact leg the hypothesis claims is welfare-gated). Skim ANRR 2019 JPE \\\"Democracy Does Cause Growth\\\" (<=10 min) only for event-study window/timing conventions.\\n\\nC. Houle (2009, World Politics) \u2014 survival/consolidation setup [15 min]\\nLocate Cambridge Core landing page + full-text PDF (search \\\"Houle inequality democracy world politics 2009 pdf\\\"; MSU faculty page hosts it; Project MUSE as fallback for abstract + first pages). Grep: \\\"hazard|survival|Weibull|Cox\\\", \\\"Gini\\\", \\\"lag|five-year|moving average\\\", \\\"control\\\", \\\"sample\\\", \\\"consolid\\\", \\\"breakdown\\\".\\nRecord: (a) outcome and sample (democracies, years, N regime breakdowns); (b) inequality operationalization (source: Deininger-Squire/WIID? interpolation? lagged moving average?); (c) model family (discrete-time? Cox? duration dependence?), controls, clustering; (d) key coefficients and interpretation for the young-democracy \\\"consolidation\\\" frame; (e) his operational definition of consolidation-as-survival (justifies the \\\"first democratic decade\\\" framing).\\n\\nD. Campante & Chor (2012, QJE) \u2014 the schooling x opportunities interaction template [20 min]\\nLocate NBER w16752 PDF (free) or Chor's faculty page; OUP QJE page for the final version. Grep: \\\"interaction\\\", \\\"schooling\\\", \\\"opportunit\\\", \\\"unemployment\\\", \\\"instrument\\\", \\\"probit|logit\\\", \\\"Arab\\\", \\\"coefficient\\\", \\\"Figure|Table\\\".\\nRecord: (a) unit of analysis (country-level and individual Arab Barometer); (b) DV coding (protest onset/intensity); (c) the two interacted regressors and their exact construction \u2014 schooling (average years / expansion) and the \\\"economic opportunities\\\" measure (components: educated-youth unemployment? income? corruption?); (d) the printed interaction equation and its sign pattern (schooling raises protest only where opportunities are poor); (e) endogeneity treatment (IV? lagged schooling growth?); (f) headline marginal effects \u2014 this calibrates the iter-2 mismatch arm (ILO educated-youth unemployment on OWID).\\n\\nE. Haggard & Kaufman (2021) \u2014 the coalitional null + case list [25 min]\\nLocate CUP book page + Google Books preview (search inside for the intro/ch.1 case summary); their Journal of Democracy pieces (\\\"Backsliding: Democratic Regress in the Contemporary World\\\", JD 2021; verify existence/title); published reviews (Perspectives on Politics, APSR) that summarize the case list; their 2016 Annual Review of Political Science \\\"Democratization During the Third Wave\\\" for welfare-state development in new democracies. Grep: \\\"backslider\\\", \\\"case\\\", \\\"Hungary\\\", \\\"Poland\\\", \\\"Turkey\\\", \\\"Venezuela\\\", \\\"South Africa\\\", \\\"inequality\\\", \\\"grievance\\\", \\\"polariz\\\", \\\"did not|no evidence\\\".\\nRecord: (a) their operational definition of backsliding (index, threshold, period); (b) the complete backslider list AND the survivors they compare against \u2014 critical for iter-2 sensitivity analysis (does the gate hold within H&K's own classification?); (c) the exact evidence for \\\"grievances/inequality did not differentiate\\\": inequality/crisis comparisons between backsliders and survivors (tables or reported numbers), and their polarization measures (V-Dem v2x_polarization? their own coding?); (d) their welfare-state material (2008 book \\\"Development, Democracy, and Welfare States\\\"; 2016 ARPS) on WHEN new democracies built welfare states \u2014 feeds the first-decade measurement design.\\n\\nF. Acemoglu, Egorov & Sonin (2013, QJE) \u2014 the pre-commitment mechanism [15 min]\\nLocate NBER w17357 PDF (free) or Acemoglu's MIT page. Grep: \\\"populist\\\", \\\"redistribut\\\", \\\"inequality\\\", \\\"Proposition\\\", \\\"equilibrium\\\", \\\"commit\\\".\\nRecord: (a) model skeleton: agents, candidates (established elite vs populist), timing, what the populist promises; (b) the \\\"endogenous limits to redistribution\\\" result \u2014 why high inequality makes elites limit redistribution and how that opens the populist equilibrium; (c) a plain-English mechanism statement usable in the paper (mechanism iii of the gate); (d) comparative statics that map to observable proxies (low pre-committed redistribution + high inequality => polarization/populist support).\\n\\nG. Policy feedback + universalism micro-evidence [20 min; trim to 10 if behind]\\nFetch abstracts/full texts as available for: Pierson (1993, World Politics) \\\"When Effect Becomes Cause\\\" (policy feedback: programs create constituencies); Campbell (2012, Annual Review of Political Science) \\\"Policy Makes Mass Politics\\\"; Korpi & Palme (1998, ASR) \\\"The Paradox of Redistribution\\\" (universalism reduces inequality more than targeting \u2014 the result justifying a universal-services, not CCT, gate measure); Skocpol (1991) \\\"Targeting within Universalism\\\"; Manacorda, Miguel & Vigorito (2011, AEJ: Applied) PANES transfers -> incumbent support (cash transfers buy incumbents, not institutions); Zucco (2013) Bolsa Familia -> Lula vote; Dolls, Fuest & Peichl (2012, J. Public Economics) automatic stabilizers absorb ~38% (EU) vs ~32% (US) of income shocks (quantifies mechanism i); CEQ / \\\"Commitment to Equity\\\" (Lustig et al.) benefit-incidence evidence that health+education spending reaches lower/middle deciles in developing countries (mechanism ii); IMF Fiscal Monitor (Oct 2017) on fiscal redistribution and the middle class. Record per item: one-line takeaway + citation + which gate mechanism (i)/(ii)/(iii) or the Cash-vs-Services corollary it supports.\\n\\nH. Supporting regime-data papers [10 min]\\nLuehrmann-Tannenberg-Lindberg (2018, Democratization): RoW typology, what qualifies as entering \\\"electoral democracy\\\", transition-year conventions. Boix-Miller-Rosato (2013, CPS): democracy definition used as sample screen; note vintage/updates. V-Dem \\\"Backsliding in the World\\\" report only if instantly accessible (2020-2023 episode context). Record: definitional thresholds + the rule for coding \\\"post-1990 democratizer\\\" (transition 1989-2005) and \\\"uninterrupted democracy\\\".\\n\\nPHASE 2 \u2014 OWID AVAILABILITY AUDIT (2:15-2:30, 15 min)\\nVerify by fetching each OWID page (pages only, NO dataset downloads): series exists, its source attribution, date coverage; record slug + caveats in notes/08_owid_audit.md:\\n(a) liberal-democracy-index (V-Dem v2x_libdem); (b) electoral-democracy-index and Regimes-of-the-World categories if hosted; (c) World Bank PIP Gini page and the WID Gini page; (d) WID top-10% share page; (e) domestic-health-expenditure-share-gdp (WHO GGHE-D) \u2014 CRITICAL: scrutinize start years (many countries have no pre-2000 values; first-decade end for 1989-95 transitions is 1999-2005); (f) government-expenditure-on-education-share-of-gdp (WB EdStats); (g) total-social-expenditure-gdp-oecd (OECD SOCX) \u2014 record any metadata on composition (pensions share) for the placebo arm; (h) political-polarization page (V-Dem v2x_polarization) \u2014 hosted?; (i) ILO education/mismatch series (unemployment by education level, especially educated youth) \u2014 hosted? If (h)/(i) are absent, that goes into follow_up_questions with OWID proxy candidates (overall youth unemployment, school enrollment).\\n\\nPHASE 3 \u2014 SYNTHESIS: THE DESIGN BRIEFING (2:30-2:50, 20 min)\\nDistill everything into the mechanism->specification mapping iteration 2 will implement. The report MUST specify, for each of the five specifications, the concrete form \u2014 variables, lags, fixed effects, clustering, sample, controls \u2014 and cite the source(s) each element comes from:\\n1. GATE INTERACTION (core): e.g., LDI_{c,t} ~ Gini_{c,t-5} + Welf_c0 + (Gini x Welf_c0) + controls, country+year FE, cluster by country; Welf_c0 = health+edu spending %GDP at first-decade end, time-invariant (pre-determined). Theory expectation: inequality effect ~0 in the high-welfare arm, strongly negative in the thin arm (use dossier B's dividend logic + dossier F's commitment logic as the theoretical underpinning; dossier G for the universalism justification).\\n2. ADJUDICATION SPEC: same sample, first WITHOUT interaction (baseline main effect \u2014 compare sign/magnitude to dossier A's Rau-Stokes estimate), then WITH interaction. This is the empirical reconciliation of Rau-Stokes (non-zero main effect) vs Haggard-Kaufman (no systematic economic grievance): the effect is concentrated in the thin-welfare arm.\\n3. EROSION-ONSET EVENT STUDIES + LEAD-LAG SEQUENCING: dynamic coefficients around erosion onset (-5..+5) estimated separately by welfare arm; sequencing tests (inequality surge and public-service retrenchment LEAD LDI decline by 3-5 years in the thin arm only; no anticipatory welfare decline before erosion in the high arm). Ground onset in dossier A's episode list and dossier H's sequencing framework; guard reverse causality using the Szikra-Oektem (2023, JESP) finding that welfare states are transformed UNDER backsliding \u2014 quick search for that paper (<=10 min) or cite from its abstract.\\n4. WELFARE-DEPENDENT DIVIDEND TEST: dossier B's ANRR event-study design restricted to post-1990 democratizers, split by first-decade welfare legacy; outcome: WID top-10% share (plus health+edu spending); then test whether first-decade dividend size predicts 2010-2022 LDI resilience.\\n5. MECHANISM ARMS: (a) polarization: v2x_polarization as outcome of Gini x thin-welfare (mediation toward LDI decline) \u2014 anchored in dossier E's emphasis on polarization as proximate cause; (b) mismatch: Campante-Chor interaction template (dossier D) transposed: schooling/education expansion x ILO educated-youth-unemployment x thin welfare on Delta-LDI, with OWID availability from Phase 2.\\nAlso produce: DIVERGENCES-TO-BRIDGE section \u2014 (i) Rau-Stokes uninterrupted-democracy sample 1995-2020 vs young democratizers 1989-2005: iter-2 must KEEP below-threshold democratizers with the continuous LDI outcome instead of censoring them; (ii) SOCX pension-heavy total spending vs the universal-services measure (health+edu) \u2014 the placebo arm; (iii) Houle's coup-era discrete survival vs graded erosion-era outcome; (iv) ANRR's 1960-2010 all-democratizations dividend vs the post-1990 wave; (v) H&K's case-based evidence vs the interaction design; and a MEASUREMENT-RISK section \u2014 PIP Gini 5-year waves/coverage in the young-democratizer sample, WHO GGHE-D pre-2000 gaps, WID interpolation, OWID vintage/anchor-year caveats \u2014 listing which items must go to follow_up_questions.\\n\\nPHASE 4 \u2014 WRITE OUTPUTS (2:50-3:00, 10 min)\\nresearch_report.md sections: 1) Executive summary (the five specs + key calibration numbers, <=1 page); 2) Per-source extraction dossier (paper -> specs with quotes + URLs); 3) Mechanism->specification mapping table; 4) Divergences & bridging decisions; 5) OWID availability audit; 6) Measurement risks and open items; 7) Full source list with URLs/DOIs (mark PAYWALLED-ABSTRACT-ONLY where applicable).\\nresearch_out.json: answer = <=800-word synthesis covering: what each study contributes, the five specs at specification level, the two must-bridge divergences, and the top-3 data risks; sources = every source actually used (title, url, doi, one-line contribution); follow_up_questions = actionable questions for iteration 2, including at minimum: (a) does OWID host V-Dem polarization and ILO educated-youth unemployment (and which proxy if not)?; (b) is Rau-Stokes replication data (episode list) public?; (c) PIP Gini coverage for post-1990 democratizers vs WID top-10 as treatment?; (d) WHO GGHE-D pre-2000 gaps \u2014 proxy with enrollment/immunization or later-start values?; (e) OECD SOCX composition confirmation for the placebo; (f) which regime classification (RoW vs BMR) defines the transition window?\\nValidate research_out.json with a JSON parser if available (python -m json.tool via the tooling venv); otherwise manual review.\\n\\nPHASE 5 \u2014 QA (final minutes, fold into Phase 4)\\n- No citation without a fetched source; no unexplained number; every number in the briefing has a URL+quote in notes.\\n- Each of the five specifications is grounded in >=1 dossier.\\n- Any item not extracted is explicitly marked UNVERIFIED in its dossier and mirrored into follow_up_questions.\\n- Outputs exist, JSON is valid, and the report is self-contained enough for iteration 2's experiment designer to implement specs 1-5 without re-reading the primary papers.\\n\\nFAILURE SCENARIOS & FALLBACKS\\n- Paywall (QJE, World Politics, ASR, CUP): NBER/author PDFs (Campante-Chor w16752, AES w17357, ANRR w19746, Houle's MSU page), Google Books preview for H&K, journal abstracts, and published reviews for case lists. Mark PAYWALLED-ABSTRACT-ONLY; extract what is available; never fabricate.\\n- PNAS SI inaccessible: work from the main text; note which tables came from the SI.\\n- Search engine failure / rate limits: switch to --mode scholarly (OpenAlex/Crossref); retry with backoff; if the ability server is down, report it rather than improvising.\\n- Time pressure: prioritize A (Rau-Stokes), B (ANRR dividend), E (H&K case list), D (Campante-Chor), then the OWID audit; trim G/H to abstracts; compress F to the mechanism statement.\\n- Conflicting metadata (e.g., ANRR venue): record both with URLs and flag for the experiment (the citation matters for the paper's references).\\n\\nCONSTRAINTS\\n- Zero LLM spend: this artifact needs NO OpenRouter calls (the $10 budget is untouched); all work uses free web tooling.\\n- All files written inside the executor's workspace (absolute paths, subdirectories notes/ OK).\\n- No dataset downloads, no data computation, no model estimation \u2014 this is pure information gathering + synthesis. The only scripts used are the web-tool search/fetch/grep helpers.\",\n  \"explanation\": \"Iteration 2's experiment can only adjudicate Rau-Stokes versus Haggard-Kaufman through the welfare gate if it faithfully reuses their exact measurement and design choices instead of reinventing them: the Rau-Stokes sample screen, erosion-episode coding, Gini source and lag, controls, model family and clustering determine whether the iteration-2 baseline main effect is comparable to the flagship finding; the ANRR event-study form, outcome list (top-decile share, public goods), and timing conventions size the welfare-dependent dividend test; Houle's survival setup justifies the first-democratic-decade framing; Campante-Chor's schooling-times-opportunities interaction is the template for the educated-youth-mismatch arm; Haggard-Kaufman's backslider/survivor case list enables the qualitative-quantitative bridge and a sensitivity analysis; Acemoglu-Egorov-Sonin's endogenous-limits-to-redistribution logic operationalizes the pre-commitment mechanism; and the policy-feedback/universalism evidence (Pierson, Campbell, Korpi-Palme, Manacorda et al., Dolls-Fuest-Peichl, CEQ incidence) supplies the micro-foundations for the constituency-formation and automatic-stabilization mechanisms as well as the cash-vs-universal-services corollary. The OWID availability audit additionally de-risks the hard constraint that everything be testable on OWID panels (LDI, PIP/WID inequality, GGHE-D and EdStats spending, SOCX placebo, polarization, ILO mismatch) before any experiment is written. The deliverable is a design briefing that maps every hypothesis mechanism to one of five concrete specifications \u2014 gate interaction, adjudication (main effect with and without interaction), erosion-onset event studies with lead-lag sequencing, the welfare-dependent dividend test, and the polarization/mismatch mechanism arms \u2014 and flags the divergences that must be bridged, most importantly the Rau-Stokes uninterrupted-democracy sample versus the young-democratizer sample and the pension-heavy SOCX composition versus the universal-services measure. The only scripts used are the web-tool search/fetch/grep helpers.\",\n  \"explanation\": \"Iteration 2's experiment can only adjudicate Rau-Stokes versus Haggard-Kaufman through the welfare gate if it faithfully reuses their exact measurement and design choices instead of reinventing them: the Rau-Stokes sample screen, erosion-episode coding, Gini source and lag, controls, model family and clustering determine whether the iteration-2 baseline main effect is comparable to the flagship finding; the ANRR event-study form, outcome list (top-decile share, public goods), and timing conventions size the welfare-dependent dividend test; Houle's survival setup justifies the first-democratic-decade framing; Campante-Chor's schooling-times-opportunities interaction is the template for the educated-youth-mismatch arm; Haggard-Kaufman's backslider/survivor case list enables the qualitative-quantitative bridge and a sensitivity analysis; Acemoglu-Egorov-Sonin's endogenous-limits-to-redistribution logic operationalizes the pre-commitment mechanism; and the policy-feedback/universalism evidence (Pierson, Campbell, Korpi-Palme, Manacorda et al., Dolls-Fuest-Peichl, CEQ incidence) supplies the micro-foundations for the constituency-formation and automatic-stabilization mechanisms as well as the cash-vs-universal-services corollary. (v) H&K's case-based evidence vs the interaction design; and a MEASUREMENT-RISK section \u2014 PIP Gini 5-year waves/coverage in the young-democratizer sample, WHO GGHE-D pre-2000 gaps, WID interpolation, OWID vintage/anchor-year caveats \u2014 listing which items must go to follow_up_questions.\\n\\nPHASE 4 \u2014 WRITE OUTPUTS (2:50-3:00, 10 min)\\nresearch_report.md sections: 1) Executive summary (the five specs + key calibration numbers, <=1 page); 2) Per-source extraction dossier (paper -> specs with quotes + URLs); 3) Mechanism->specification mapping table; 4) Divergences & bridging decisions; 5) OWID availability audit; 6) Measurement risks and open items; 7) Full source list with URLs/DOIs (mark PAYWALLED-ABSTRACT-ONLY where applicable).\\nresearch_out.json: answer = <=800-word synthesis covering: what each study contributes, the five specs at specification level, the two must-bridge divergences, and the top-3 data risks; sources = every source actually used (title, url, doi, one-line contribution); follow_up_questions = actionable questions for iteration 2, including at minimum: (a) does OWID host V-Dem polarization and ILO educated-youth unemployment (and which proxy if not)?; (b) is Rau-Stokes replication data (episode list) public?; (c) PIP Gini coverage for post-1990 democratizers vs WID top-10 as treatment?; (d) WHO GGHE-D pre-2000 gaps \u2014 proxy with enrollment/immunization or later-start values?; (e) OECD SOCX composition confirmation for the placebo; (f) which regime classification (RoW vs BMR) defines the transition window?\\nValidate research_out.json with a JSON parser if available (python -m json.tool via the tooling venv); otherwise manual review.\\n\\nPHASE 5 \u2014 QA (final minutes, fold into Phase 4)\\n- No citation without a fetched source; no unexplained number; every number in the briefing has a URL+quote in notes.\\n- Each of the five specifications is grounded in >=1 dossier.\\n- Any item not extracted is explicitly marked UNVERIFIED in its dossier and mirrored into follow_up_questions.\\n- Outputs exist, JSON is valid, and the report is self-contained enough for iteration 2's experiment designer to implement specs 1-5 without re-reading the primary papers.\\n\\nFAILURE SCENARIOS & FALLBACKS\\n- Paywall (QJE, World Politics, ASR, CUP): NBER/author PDFs (Campante-Chor w16752, AES w17357, ANRR w19746, Houle's MSU page), Google Books preview for H&K, journal abstracts, and published reviews for case lists. Mark PAYWALLED-ABSTRACT-ONLY; extract what is available; never fabricate.\\n- PNAS SI inaccessible: work from the main text; note which tables came from the SI.\\n- Search engine failure / rate limits: switch to --mode scholarly (OpenAlex/Crossref); retry with backoff; if the ability server is down, report it rather than improvising.\\n- Time pressure: prioritize A (Rau-Stokes), B (ANRR dividend), E (H&K case list), D (Campante-Chor), then the OWID audit; trim G/H to abstracts; compress F to the mechanism statement.\\n- Conflicting metadata (e.g., ANRR venue): record both with URLs and flag for the experiment (the citation matters for the paper's references).\\n\\nCONSTRAINTS\\n- Zero LLM spend: this artifact needs NO OpenRouter calls (the $10 budget is untouched); all work uses free web tooling.\\n- All files written inside the executor's workspace (absolute paths, subdirectories notes/ OK).\\n- No dataset downloads, no data computation, no model estimation \u2014 this is pure information gathering + synthesis. The only scripts used are the web-tool search/fetch/grep helpers.\",\n  \"explanation\": \"Iteration 2's experiment can only adjudicate Rau-Stokes versus Haggard-Kaufman through the welfare gate if it faithfully reuses their exact measurement and design choices instead of reinventing them: the Rau-Stokes sample screen, erosion-episode coding, Gini source and lag, controls, model family and clustering determine whether the iteration-2 baseline main effect is comparable to the flagship finding; the ANRR event-study form, outcome list (top-decile share, public goods), and timing conventions size the welfare-dependent dividend test; Houle's survival setup justifies the first-democratic-decade framing; Campante-Chor's schooling-times-opportunities interaction is the template for the educated-youth-mismatch arm; Haggard-Kaufman's backslider/survivor case list enables the qualitative-quantitative bridge and a sensitivity analysis; Acemoglu-Egorov-Sonin's endogenous-limits-to-redistribution logic operationalizes the pre-commitment mechanism; and the policy-feedback/universalism evidence (Pierson, Campbell, Korpi-Palme, Manacorda et al., Dolls-Fuest-Peichl, CEQ incidence) supplies the micro-foundations for the constituency-formation and automatic-stabilization mechanisms as well as the cash-vs-universal-services corollary. The OWID availability audit additionally de-risks the hard constraint that everything be testable on OWID panels (LDI, PIP/WID inequality, GGHE-D and EdStats spending, SOCX placebo, polarization, ILO mismatch) before any experiment is written. The deliverable is a design briefing that maps every hypothesis mechanism to one of five concrete specifications \u2014 gate interaction, adjudication (main effect with and without interaction), erosion-onset event studies with lead-lag sequencing, the welfare-dependent dividend test, and the polarization/mismatch mechanism arms \u2014 and flags the divergences that must be bridged, most importantly the Rau-Stokes uninterrupted-democracy sample versus the young-democratizer sample and the pension-heavy SOCX composition versus the universal-services measure. Without this briefing, the experiment would risk either replicating a main effect that everyone already knows or testing a gate whose operationalizations no longer connect to the literature it claims to reconcile.\",\n  }", "new_content": "{\n  \"title\": \"Mine key studies to design the welfare-gate test\",\n  \"summary\": \"Web-research the six load-bearing prior works (Rau-Stokes 2025; Acemoglu-Naidu-Restrepo-Robinson 2015; Houle 2009; Campante-Chor 2012; Haggard-Kaufman 2021; Acemoglu-Egorov-Sonin 2013) plus policy-feedback/universalism micro-evidence and the OWID series inventory, and distill them into a design briefing that maps every mechanism of the welfare-gate hypothesis to a concrete regression specification iteration 2 can run on OWID panels.\",\n  \"runpod_compute_profile\": \"cpu_light\",\n  \"question\": \"What exact samples, model specifications, variable operationalizations, controls, clustering choices, periods, and case lists do the core prior studies use (Rau & Stokes 2025 PNAS; Acemoglu, Naidu, Restrepo & Robinson 2015; Houle 2009; Campante & Chor 2012; Haggard & Kaufman 2021; Acemoglu, Egorov & Sonin 2013; plus the policy-feedback and universalism literatures), and how must each be translated into the gate-interaction, adjudication, erosion-onset event-study, welfare-dependent dividend, and mechanism-arm specifications for the OWID panel of post-1990 democratizers, flagging the divergences the gate design must bridge?\",\n  \"research_plan\": \"STEP-BY-STEP RESEARCH PLAN (3 h budget; pure web research; zero LLM spend \u2014 no OpenRouter calls required for this artifact; everything free via web tooling).\\n\\nROLE & DELIVERABLES\\nYou are the RESEARCH executor. Produce exactly two outputs inside your workspace:\\n1. research_report.md \u2014 the DESIGN BRIEFING (main deliverable; structure in Phase 4).\\n2. research_out.json \u2014 {\\\"answer\\\": <concise synthesis>, \\\"sources\\\": [{\\\"title\\\",\\\"url\\\",\\\"doi\\\",\\\"contribution\\\"}], \\\"follow_up_questions\\\": [...]}.\\nSave per-source extraction notes under notes/ as you go (notes/01_rau_stokes.md, 02_anrr.md, 03_houle.md, 04_campante_chor.md, 05_haggard_kaufman.md, 06_aes_populism.md, 07_policy_feedback.md, 08_owid_audit.md, 09_datasets.md, 00_sources.md) so nothing is lost on a timeout.\\n\\nTOOLING (read the aii-web-tools skill first)\\n- Use built-in WebSearch/WebFetch if available (load once via ToolSearch(\\\"select:WebSearch,WebFetch\\\") if they appear as deferred tools). Otherwise use the skill scripts: aii_fast_web_search.py --query \\\"...\\\" --mode scholarly (OpenAlex/Crossref for DOIs and reliable metadata) and --mode general (discovery); aii_fast_web_fetch.py fetch --url ... --max-chars N and grep --url ... --pattern \\\"regex\\\" -i for exact text/numbers in long pages and PDFs.\\n- Workflow per source: search (scholarly) -> fetch (gist) -> grep (exact equations, numbers, case lists). Grep PDFs with context windows (~200-300 chars).\\n- Parallelize aggressively: launch 4-6 independent searches/fetches per turn; only sequence fetch-after-search. If the ability server is down, say so in the report rather than improvising.\\n- VERIFICATION RULE: every number, case, equation, and quote you record must trace to a fetched source (URL + snippet in your notes). Never guess citations or DOIs \u2014 get them from Crossref/OpenAlex or the publisher page. If a source is paywalled, mark it PAYWALLED-ABSTRACT-ONLY, extract what the abstract/Google Books/review gives, do NOT invent details, and route the missing items to follow_up_questions.\\n\\nPHASE 0 \u2014 DISCOVERY & SOURCE LOCK (0:00-0:15)\\nRun one scholarly-mode search per target, in parallel, to lock exact titles/authors/venues/years/DOIs/URLs:\\n1. \\\"Rau Stokes income inequality erosion democracy PNAS\\\"\\n2. \\\"Acemoglu Naidu Restrepo Robinson democracy redistribution inequality\\\"\\n3. \\\"Houle inequality and democracy why inequality harms consolidation but not democratization\\\"\\n4. \\\"Campante Chor why was the Arab world poised for revolution schooling economic opportunities\\\"\\n5. \\\"Acemoglu Egorov Sonin a political theory of populism\\\"\\n6. \\\"Haggard Kaufman backsliding democratic regress in the contemporary world\\\"\\n7. \\\"Korpi Palme paradox of redistribution strategies of equality\\\"\\n8. \\\"Pierson when effect becomes cause policy feedback and political change\\\"\\nFor each: record in notes/00_sources.md a status column (FOUND / FETCHED / EXTRACTED / PAYWALLED-ABSTRACT-ONLY) plus a fetchable URL: PNAS article page + its Supplementary PDF (pnas.*.sapp.pdf); NBER working-paper PDFs; author-hosted PDFs (Houle's MSU faculty page, Chor's faculty page, Acemoglu's MIT page); publisher landing pages (OUP QJE, Cambridge WP, SAGE JESP, Annual Reviews); Google Books preview for the CUP book. Also confirm two supporting dataset papers: Luehrmann-Tannenberg-Lindberg \\\"Regimes of the World\\\" (Democratization, 2018) and Boix-Miller-Rosato \\\"A Complete Data Set of Political Regimes, 1800-2007\\\" (CPS, 2013) \u2014 these define transition dates and the \\\"uninterrupted democracy\\\" sample the experiment inherits.\\nNOTE: the hypothesis cites ANRR 2015 as \\\"AER\\\"; expected reality is a Handbook of Income Distribution Vol. 2 (Elsevier, 2015) chapter (NBER w19746 is the working paper). VERIFY the true venue via Crossref/OpenAlex and record both citations.\\n\\nPHASE 1 \u2014 EXTRACTION DOSSIERS (0:15-2:15, ~120 min)\\nPer workstream: fetch main text -> grep exact items -> record into the numbered notes file. Priorities when short on time: A, B, E, D are load-bearing; trim G/H first; F can compress to mechanism statement.\\n\\nA. Rau & Stokes (2025, PNAS) \u2014 the flagship inequality->erosion main effect [25 min]\\nLocate via PNAS DOI search + SI PDF. Grep patterns: \\\"uninterrupted\\\", \\\"sample\\\", \\\"erosion\\\", \\\"episode\\\", \\\"Gini|SWIID|PIP|inequality\\\", \\\"hazard|Cox|logit|OLS|probit\\\", \\\"control\\\", \\\"cluster\\\", \\\"lag\\\", \\\"1995|2000|2020\\\", \\\"threshold\\\".\\nRecord: (a) exact sample definition \u2014 which regime classification screens countries, which countries are excluded (post-1990 democratizers that fell below the democracy threshold?), N countries, N country-years, period; (b) the full erosion-episode list and coding rule (binary onset? sustained V-Dem LDI drop? threshold?); (c) inequality variable: exact source (SWIID/PIP/WID), transformation, lag structure; (d) full control set; (e) model family, fixed effects, clustering; (f) headline coefficients/SEs (needed to calibrate the iter-2 main-effect magnitude); (g) whether they run ANY moderation/interaction analysis (expected: none \u2014 that gap is the gate); (h) mention of replication-data availability. Also spend <=5 min searching for published commentary/replication critiques of the paper.\\n\\nB. Acemoglu, Naidu, Restrepo & Robinson (2015) \u2014 the democratization-dividend design [30 min]\\nLocate NBER w19746 PDF (free) + published-version landing page (ScienceDirect Handbook chapter; verify venue per Phase 0 note). Grep patterns: \\\"democratiz\\\", \\\"event stud\\\", \\\"top decile|top 10|income share\\\", \\\"school|education\\\", \\\"tax\\\", \\\"public good\\\", \\\"matching\\\", \\\"1960\\\", \\\"equation\\\", \\\"cluster\\\", \\\"sample\\\".\\nRecord: (a) democratization-event definition and regime-classification source; (b) sample N countries and years; (c) full outcome list \u2014 top-decile income share (from WID?), tax/GDP share, social/education/health spending, schooling; (d) estimation design: OLS with country+year FE, dynamic leads/lags event-study form, any matching estimator \u2014 write out their actual equation(s) as printed; (e) controls and clustering; (f) headline magnitudes: effect of democratization on top-decile share at +10 years, on public-goods measures (these size the iter-2 welfare-dependent dividend test); (g) their own caveats about the inequality leg (the exact leg the hypothesis claims is welfare-gated). Skim ANRR 2019 JPE \\\"Democracy Does Cause Growth\\\" (<=10 min) only for event-study window/timing conventions.\\n\\nC. Houle (2009, World Politics) \u2014 survival/consolidation setup [15 min]\\nLocate Cambridge Core landing page + full-text PDF (search \\\"Houle inequality democracy world politics 2009 pdf\\\"; MSU faculty page hosts it; Project MUSE as fallback for abstract + first pages). Grep: \\\"hazard|survival|Weibull|Cox\\\", \\\"Gini\\\", \\\"lag|five-year|moving average\\\", \\\"control\\\", \\\"sample\\\", \\\"consolid\\\", \\\"breakdown\\\".\\nRecord: (a) outcome and sample (democracies, years, N regime breakdowns); (b) inequality operationalization (source: Deininger-Squire/WIID? interpolation? lagged moving average?); (c) model family (discrete-time? Cox? duration dependence?), controls, clustering; (d) key coefficients and interpretation for the young-democracy \\\"consolidation\\\" frame; (e) his operational definition of consolidation-as-survival (justifies the \\\"first democratic decade\\\" framing).\\n\\nD. Campante & Chor (2012, QJE) \u2014 the schooling x opportunities interaction template [20 min]\\nLocate NBER w16752 PDF (free) or Chor's faculty page; OUP QJE page for the final version. Grep: \\\"interaction\\\", \\\"schooling\\\", \\\"opportunit\\\", \\\"unemployment\\\", \\\"instrument\\\", \\\"probit|logit\\\", \\\"Arab\\\", \\\"coefficient\\\", \\\"Figure|Table\\\".\\nRecord: (a) unit of analysis (country-level and individual Arab Barometer); (b) DV coding (protest onset/intensity); (c) the two interacted regressors and their exact construction \u2014 schooling (average years / expansion) and the \\\"economic opportunities\\\" measure (components: educated-youth unemployment? income? corruption?); (d) the printed interaction equation and its sign pattern (schooling raises protest only where opportunities are poor); (e) endogeneity treatment (IV? lagged schooling growth?); (f) headline marginal effects \u2014 this calibrates the iter-2 mismatch arm (ILO educated-youth unemployment on OWID).\\n\\nE. Haggard & Kaufman (2021) \u2014 the coalitional null + case list [25 min]\\nLocate CUP book page + Google Books preview (search inside for the intro/ch.1 case summary); their Journal of Democracy pieces (\\\"Backsliding: Democratic Regress in the Contemporary World\\\", JD 2021; verify existence/title); published reviews (Perspectives on Politics, APSR) that summarize the case list; their 2016 Annual Review of Political Science \\\"Democratization During the Third Wave\\\" for welfare-state development in new democracies. Grep: \\\"backslider\\\", \\\"case\\\", \\\"Hungary\\\", \\\"Poland\\\", \\\"Turkey\\\", \\\"Venezuela\\\", \\\"South Africa\\\", \\\"inequality\\\", \\\"grievance\\\", \\\"polariz\\\", \\\"did not|no evidence\\\".\\nRecord: (a) their operational definition of backsliding (index, threshold, period); (b) the complete backslider list AND the survivors they compare against \u2014 critical for iter-2 sensitivity analysis (does the gate hold within H&K's own classification?); (c) the exact evidence for \\\"grievances/inequality did not differentiate\\\": inequality/crisis comparisons between backsliders and survivors (tables or reported numbers), and their polarization measures (V-Dem v2x_polarization? their own coding?); (d) their welfare-state material (2008 book \\\"Development, Democracy, and Welfare States\\\"; 2016 ARPS) on WHEN new democracies built welfare states \u2014 feeds the first-decade measurement design.\\n\\nF. Acemoglu, Egorov & Sonin (2013, QJE) \u2014 the pre-commitment mechanism [15 min]\\nLocate NBER w17357 PDF (free) or Acemoglu's MIT page. Grep: \\\"populist\\\", \\\"redistribut\\\", \\\"inequality\\\", \\\"Proposition\\\", \\\"equilibrium\\\", \\\"commit\\\".\\nRecord: (a) model skeleton: agents, candidates (established elite vs populist), timing, what the populist promises; (b) the \\\"endogenous limits to redistribution\\\" result \u2014 why high inequality makes elites limit redistribution and how that opens the populist equilibrium; (c) a plain-English mechanism statement usable in the paper (mechanism iii of the gate); (d) comparative statics that map to observable proxies (low pre-committed redistribution + high inequality => polarization/populist support).\\n\\nG. Policy feedback + universalism micro-evidence [20 min; trim to 10 if behind]\\nFetch abstracts/full texts as available for: Pierson (1993, World Politics) \\\"When Effect Becomes Cause\\\" (policy feedback: programs create constituencies); Campbell (2012, Annual Review of Political Science) \\\"Policy Makes Mass Politics\\\"; Korpi & Palme (1998, ASR) \\\"The Paradox of Redistribution\\\" (universalism reduces inequality more than targeting \u2014 the result justifying a universal-services, not CCT, gate measure); Skocpol (1991) \\\"Targeting within Universalism\\\"; Manacorda, Miguel & Vigorito (2011, AEJ: Applied) PANES transfers -> incumbent support (cash transfers buy incumbents, not institutions); Zucco (2013) Bolsa Familia -> Lula vote; Dolls, Fuest & Peichl (2012, J. Public Economics) automatic stabilizers absorb ~38% (EU) vs ~32% (US) of income shocks (quantifies mechanism i); CEQ / \\\"Commitment to Equity\\\" (Lustig et al.) benefit-incidence evidence that health+education spending reaches lower/middle deciles in developing countries (mechanism ii); IMF Fiscal Monitor (Oct 2017) on fiscal redistribution and the middle class. Record per item: one-line takeaway + citation + which gate mechanism (i)/(ii)/(iii) or the Cash-vs-Services corollary it supports.\\n\\nH. Supporting regime-data papers [10 min]\\nLuehrmann-Tannenberg-Lindberg (2018, Democratization): RoW typology, what qualifies as entering \\\"electoral democracy\\\", transition-year conventions. Boix-Miller-Rosato (2013, CPS): democracy definition used as sample screen; note vintage/updates. V-Dem \\\"Backsliding in the World\\\" report only if instantly accessible (2020-2023 episode context). Record: definitional thresholds + the rule for coding \\\"post-1990 democratizer\\\" (transition 1989-2005) and \\\"uninterrupted democracy\\\".\\n\\nPHASE 2 \u2014 OWID AVAILABILITY AUDIT (2:15-2:30, 15 min)\\nVerify by fetching each OWID page (pages only, NO dataset downloads): series exists, its source attribution, date coverage; record slug + caveats in notes/08_owid_audit.md:\\n(a) liberal-democracy-index (V-Dem v2x_libdem); (b) electoral-democracy-index and Regimes-of-the-World categories if hosted; (c) World Bank PIP Gini page and the WID Gini page; (d) WID top-10% share page; (e) domestic-health-expenditure-share-gdp (WHO GGHE-D) \u2014 CRITICAL: scrutinize start years (many countries have no pre-2000 values; first-decade end for 1989-95 transitions is 1999-2005); (f) government-expenditure-on-education-share-of-gdp (WB EdStats); (g) total-social-expenditure-gdp-oecd (OECD SOCX) \u2014 record any metadata on composition (pensions share) for the placebo arm; (h) political-polarization page (V-Dem v2x_polarization) \u2014 hosted?; (i) ILO education/mismatch series (unemployment by education level, especially educated youth) \u2014 hosted? If (h)/(i) are absent, that goes into follow_up_questions with OWID proxy candidates (overall youth unemployment, school enrollment).\\n\\nPHASE 3 \u2014 SYNTHESIS: THE DESIGN BRIEFING (2:30-2:50, 20 min)\\nDistill everything into the mechanism->specification mapping iteration 2 will implement. The report MUST specify, for each of the five specifications, the concrete form \u2014 variables, lags, fixed effects, clustering, sample, controls \u2014 and cite the source(s) each element comes from:\\n1. GATE INTERACTION (core): e.g., LDI_{c,t} ~ Gini_{c,t-5} + Welf_c0 + (Gini x Welf_c0) + controls, country+year FE, cluster by country; Welf_c0 = health+edu spending %GDP at first-decade end, time-invariant (pre-determined). Theory expectation: inequality effect ~0 in the high-welfare arm, strongly negative in the thin arm (use dossier B's dividend logic + dossier F's commitment logic as the theoretical underpinning; dossier G for the universalism justification) [VERIFIED-INTACT].\\n2. ADJUDICATION SPEC: same sample, first WITHOUT interaction (baseline main effect \u2014 compare sign/magnitude to dossier A's Rau-Stokes estimate), then WITH interaction. This is the empirical reconciliation of Rau-Stokes (non-zero main effect) vs Haggard-Kaufman (no systematic economic grievance): the effect is concentrated in the thin-welfare arm.\\n3. EROSION-ONSET EVENT STUDIES + LEAD-LAG SEQUENCING: dynamic coefficients around erosion onset (-5..+5) estimated separately by welfare arm; sequencing tests (inequality surge and public-service retrenchment LEAD LDI decline by 3-5 years in the thin arm only; no anticipatory welfare decline before erosion in the high arm). Ground onset in dossier A's episode list and dossier H's sequencing framework; guard reverse causality using the Szikra-Oektem (2023, JESP) finding that welfare states are transformed UNDER backsliding \u2014 quick search for that paper (<=10 min) or cite from its abstract.\\n4. WELFARE-DEPENDENT DIVIDEND TEST: dossier B's ANRR event-study design restricted to post-1990 democratizers, split by first-decade welfare legacy; outcome: WID top-10% share (plus health+edu spending); then test whether first-decade dividend size predicts 2010-2022 LDI resilience.\\n5. MECHANISM ARMS: (a) polarization: v2x_polarization as outcome of Gini x thin-welfare (mediation toward LDI decline) \u2014 anchored in dossier E's emphasis on polarization as proximate cause; (b) mismatch: Campante-Chor interaction template (dossier D) transposed: schooling/education expansion x ILO educated-youth-unemployment x thin welfare on Delta-LDI, with OWID availability from Phase 2.\\nAlso produce: DIVERGENCES-TO-BRIDGE section \u2014 (i) Rau-Stokes uninterrupted-democracy sample 1995-2020 vs young democratizers 1989-2005: iter-2 must KEEP below-threshold democratizers with the continuous LDI outcome instead of censoring them; The only scripts used are the web-tool search/fetch/grep helpers.\",\n  \"explanation\": \"Iteration 2's experiment can only adjudicate Rau-Stokes versus Haggard-Kaufman through the welfare gate if it faithfully reuses their exact measurement and design choices instead of reinventing them: the Rau-Stokes sample screen, erosion-episode coding, Gini source and lag, controls, model family and clustering determine whether the iteration-2 baseline main effect is comparable to the flagship finding; the ANRR event-study form, outcome list (top-decile share, public goods), and timing conventions size the welfare-dependent dividend test; Houle's survival setup justifies the first-democratic-decade framing; Campante-Chor's schooling-times-opportunities interaction is the template for the educated-youth-mismatch arm; Haggard-Kaufman's backslider/survivor case list enables the qualitative-quantitative bridge and a sensitivity analysis; Acemoglu-Egorov-Sonin's endogenous-limits-to-redistribution logic operationalizes the pre-commitment mechanism; and the policy-feedback/universalism evidence (Pierson, Campbell, Korpi-Palme, Manacorda et al., Dolls-Fuest-Peichl, CEQ incidence) supplies the micro-foundations for the constituency-formation and automatic-stabilization mechanisms as well as the cash-vs-universal-services corollary. The OWID availability audit additionally de-risks the hard constraint that everything be testable on OWID panels (LDI, PIP/WID inequality, GGHE-D and EdStats spending, SOCX placebo, polarization, ILO mismatch) before any experiment is written. The deliverable is a design briefing that maps every hypothesis mechanism to one of five concrete specifications \u2014 gate interaction, adjudication (main effect with and without interaction), erosion-onset event studies with lead-lag sequencing, the welfare-dependent dividend test, and the polarization/mismatch mechanism arms \u2014 and flags the divergences that must be bridged, most importantly the Rau-Stokes uninterrupted-democracy sample versus the young-democratizer sample and the pension-heavy SOCX composition versus the universal-services measure. Without this briefing, the experiment would risk either replicating a main effect that everyone already knows or testing a gate whose operationalizations no longer connect to the literature it claims to reconcile.\",\n  }", "kind": "FileEditorObservation"}

Public Economics) automatic stabilizers absorb ~38% (EU) vs ~32% (US) of income shocks (quantifies mechanism i); CEQ / \\\"Commitment to Equity\\\" (Lustig et al.) benefit-incidence evidence that health+education spending reaches lower/middle deciles in developing countries (mechanism ii); IMF Fiscal Monitor (Oct 2017) on fiscal redistribution and the middle class. Record per item: one-line takeaway + citation + which gate mechanism (i)/(ii)/(iii) or the Cash-vs-Services corollary it supports.\\n\\nH. If (h)/(i) are absent, that goes into follow_up_questions with OWID proxy candidates (overall youth unemployment, school enrollment).\\n\\nPHASE 3 \u2014 SYNTHESIS: THE DESIGN BRIEFING (2:30-2:50, 20 min)\\nDistill everything into the mechanism->specification mapping iteration 2 will implement. The report MUST specify, for each of the five specifications, the concrete form \u2014 variables, lags, fixed effects, clustering, sample, controls \u2014 and cite the source(s) each element comes from:\\n1. GATE INTERACTION (core): e.g., LDI_{c,t} ~ Gini_{c,t-5} + Welf_c0 + (Gini x Welf_c0) + controls, country+year FE, cluster by country; Welf_c0 = health+edu spending %GDP at first-decade end, time-invariant (pre-determined). Theory expectation: inequality effect ~0 in the high-welfare arm, strongly negative in the thin arm (use dossier B's dividend logic + dossier F's commitment logic as the theoretical underpinning; dossier G for the universalism justification) [VERIFIED-INTACT].\\n2. ADJUDICATION SPEC: same sample, first WITHOUT interaction (baseline main effect \u2014 compare sign/magnitude to dossier A's Rau-Stokes estimate), then WITH interaction. This is the empirical reconciliation of Rau-Stokes (non-zero main effect) vs Haggard-Kaufman (no systematic economic grievance): the effect is concentrated in the thin-welfare arm.\\n3. EROSION-ONSET EVENT STUDIES + LEAD-LAG SEQUENCING: dynamic coefficients around erosion onset (-5..+5) estimated separately by welfare arm; sequencing tests (inequality surge and public-service retrenchment LEAD LDI decline by 3-5 years in the thin arm only; no anticipatory welfare decline before erosion in the high arm). Ground onset in dossier A's episode list and dossier H's sequencing framework; guard reverse causality using the Szikra-Oektem (2023, JESP) finding that welfare states are transformed UNDER backsliding \u2014 quick search for that paper (<=10 min) or cite from its abstract.\\n4. WELFARE-DEPENDENT DIVIDEND TEST: dossier B's ANRR event-study design restricted to post-1990 democratizers, split by first-decade welfare legacy; outcome: WID top-10% share (plus health+edu spending); then test whether first-decade dividend size predicts 2010-2022 LDI resilience.\\n5. MECHANISM ARMS: (a) polarization: v2x_polarization as outcome of Gini x thin-welfare (mediation toward LDI decline) \u2014 anchored in dossier E's emphasis on polarization as proximate cause; (b) mismatch: Campante-Chor interaction template (dossier D) transposed: schooling/education expansion x ILO educated-youth-unemployment x thin welfare on Delta-LDI, with OWID availability from Phase 2.\\nAlso produce: DIVERGENCES-TO-BRIDGE section \u2014 (i) Rau-Stokes uninterrupted-democracy sample 1995-2020 vs young democratizers 1989-2005: iter-2 must KEEP below-threshold democratizers with the continuous LDI outcome instead of censoring them; (ii) SOCX pension-heavy total spending vs the universal-services measure (health+edu) \u2014 the placebo arm; (iii) Houle's coup-era discrete survival vs graded erosion-era outcome; (iv) ANRR's 1960-2010 all-democratizations dividend vs the post-1990 wave; (v) H&K's case-based evidence vs the interaction design; and a MEASUREMENT-RISK section \u2014 PIP Gini 5-year waves/coverage in the young-democratizer sample, WHO GGHE-D pre-2000 gaps, WID interpolation, OWID vintage/anchor-year caveats \u2014 listing which items must go to follow_up_questions.\\n\\nPHASE 4 \u2014 WRITE OUTPUTS (2:50-3:00, 10 min)\\nresearch_report.md sections: 1) Executive summary (the five specs + key calibration numbers, <=1 page); 2) Per-source extraction dossier (paper -> specs with quotes + URLs); 3) Mechanism->specification mapping table; 4) Divergences & bridging decisions; 5) OWID availability audit; 6) Measurement risks and open items; 7) Full source list with URLs/DOIs (mark PAYWALLED-ABSTRACT-ONLY where applicable).\\nresearch_out.json: answer = <=800-word synthesis covering: what each study contributes, the five specs at specification level, the two must-bridge divergences, and the top-3 data risks; sources = every source actually used (title, url, doi, one-line contribution); follow_up_questions = actionable questions for iteration 2, including at minimum: (a) does OWID host V-Dem polarization and ILO educated-youth unemployment (and which proxy if not)?; (b) is Rau-Stokes replication data (episode list) public?; (c) PIP Gini coverage for post-1990 democratizers vs WID top-10 as treatment?; (d) WHO GGHE-D pre-2000 gaps \u2014 proxy with enrollment/immunization or later-start values?; (e) OECD SOCX composition confirmation for the placebo; (f) which regime classification (RoW vs BMR) defines the transition window?\\nValidate research_out.json with a JSON parser if available (python -m json.tool via the tooling venv); otherwise manual review.\\n\\nPHASE 5 \u2014 QA (final minutes, fold into Phase 4)\\n- No citation without a fetched source; no unexplained number; every number in the briefing has a URL+quote in notes.\\n- Each of the five specifications is grounded in >=1 dossier.\\n- Any item not extracted is explicitly marked UNVERIFIED in its dossier and mirrored into follow_up_questions.\\n- Outputs exist, JSON is valid, and the report is self-contained enough for iteration 2's experiment designer to implement specs 1-5 without re-reading the primary papers.\\n\\nFAILURE SCENARIOS & FALLBACKS\\n- Paywall (QJE, World Politics, ASR, CUP): NBER/author PDFs (Campante-Chor w16752, AES w17357, ANRR w19746, Houle's MSU page), Google Books preview for H&K, journal abstracts, and published reviews for case lists. Mark PAYWALLED-ABSTRACT-ONLY; extract what is available; never fabricate.\\n- PNAS SI inaccessible: work from the main text; note which tables came from the SI.\\n- Search engine failure / rate limits: switch to --mode scholarly (OpenAlex/Crossref); retry with backoff; if the ability server is down, report it rather than improvising.\\n- Time pressure: prioritize A (Rau-Stokes), B (ANRR dividend), E (H&K case list), D (Campante-Chor), then the OWID audit; trim G/H to abstracts; compress F to the mechanism statement.\\n- Conflicting metadata (e.g., ANRR venue): record both with URLs and flag for the experiment (the citation matters for the paper's references).\\n\\nCONSTRAINTS\\n- Zero LLM spend: this artifact needs NO OpenRouter calls (the $10 budget is untouched); all work uses free web tooling.\\n- All files written inside the executor's workspace (absolute paths, subdirectories notes/ OK).\\n- No dataset downloads, no data computation, no model estimation \u2014 this is pure information gathering + synthesis. The only scripts used are the web-tool search/fetch/grep helpers.\",\n  \"explanation\": \"Iteration 2's experiment can only adjudicate Rau-Stokes versus Haggard-Kaufman through the welfare gate if it faithfully reuses their exact measurement and design choices instead of reinventing them: the Rau-Stokes sample screen, erosion-episode coding, Gini source and lag, controls, model family and clustering determine whether the iteration-2 baseline main effect is comparable to the flagship finding; the ANRR event-study form, outcome list (top-decile share, public goods), and timing conventions size the welfare-dependent dividend test; Houle's survival setup justifies the first-democratic-decade framing; Campante-Chor's schooling-times-opportunities interaction is the template for the educated-youth-mismatch arm; Haggard-Kaufman's backslider/survivor case list enables the qualitative-quantitative bridge and a sensitivity analysis; Acemoglu-Egorov-Sonin's endogenous-limits-to-redistribution logic operationalizes the pre-commitment mechanism; and the policy-feedback/universalism evidence (Pierson, Campbell, Korpi-Palme, Manacorda et al., Dolls-Fuest-Peichl, CEQ incidence) supplies the micro-foundations for the constituency-formation and automatic-stabilization mechanisms as well as the cash-vs-universal-services corollary. The OWID availability audit additionally de-risks the hard constraint that everything be testable on OWID panels (LDI, PIP/WID inequality, GGHE-D and EdStats spending, SOCX placebo, polarization, ILO mismatch) before any experiment is written. The deliverable is a design briefing that maps every hypothesis mechanism to one of five concrete specifications \u2014 gate interaction, adjudication (main effect with and without interaction), erosion-onset event studies with lead-lag sequencing, the welfare-dependent dividend test, and the polarization/mismatch mechanism arms \u2014 and flags the divergences that must be bridged, most importantly the Rau-Stokes uninterrupted-democracy sample versus the young-democratizer sample and the pension-heavy SOCX composition versus the universal-services measure. If (h)/(i) are absent, that goes into follow_up_questions with OWID proxy candidates (overall youth unemployment, school enrollment).\\n\\nPHASE 3 \u2014 SYNTHESIS: THE DESIGN BRIEFING (2:30-2:50, 20 min)\\nDistill everything into the mechanism->specification mapping iteration 2 will implement. The report MUST specify, for each of the five specifications, the concrete form \u2014 variables, lags, fixed effects, clustering, sample, controls \u2014 and cite the source(s) each element comes from:\\n1. GATE INTERACTION (core): e.g., LDI_{c,t} ~ Gini_{c,t-5} + Welf_c0 + (Gini x Welf_c0) + controls, country+year FE, cluster by country; Welf_c0 = health+edu spending %GDP at first-decade end, time-invariant (pre-determined). Theory expectation: inequality effect ~0 in the high-welfare arm, strongly negative in the thin arm (use dossier B's dividend logic + dossier F's commitment logic as the theoretical underpinning; dossier G for the universalism justification).\\n2. ADJUDICATION SPEC: same sample, first WITHOUT interaction (baseline main effect \u2014 compare sign/magnitude to dossier A's Rau-Stokes estimate), then WITH interaction. This is the empirical reconciliation of Rau-Stokes (non-zero main effect) vs Haggard-Kaufman (no systematic economic grievance): the effect is concentrated in the thin-welfare arm.\\n3. EROSION-ONSET EVENT STUDIES + LEAD-LAG SEQUENCING: dynamic coefficients around erosion onset (-5..+5) estimated separately by welfare arm; sequencing tests (inequality surge and public-service retrenchment LEAD LDI decline by 3-5 years in the thin arm only; no anticipatory welfare decline before erosion in the high arm). Ground onset in dossier A's episode list and dossier H's sequencing framework; guard reverse causality using the Szikra-Oektem (2023, JESP) finding that welfare states are transformed UNDER backsliding \u2014 quick search for that paper (<=10 min) or cite from its abstract.\\n4. WELFARE-DEPENDENT DIVIDEND TEST: dossier B's ANRR event-study design restricted to post-1990 democratizers, split by first-decade welfare legacy; outcome: WID top-10% share (plus health+edu spending); then test whether first-decade dividend size predicts 2010-2022 LDI resilience.\\n5. MECHANISM ARMS: (a) polarization: v2x_polarization as outcome of Gini x thin-welfare (mediation toward LDI decline) \u2014 anchored in dossier E's emphasis on polarization as proximate cause; (b) mismatch: Campante-Chor interaction template (dossier D) transposed: schooling/education expansion x ILO educated-youth-unemployment x thin welfare on Delta-LDI, with OWID availability from Phase 2.\\nAlso produce: DIVERGENCES-TO-BRIDGE section \u2014 (i) Rau-Stokes uninterrupted-democracy sample 1995-2020 vs young democratizers 1989-2005: iter-2 must KEEP below-threshold democratizers with the continuous LDI outcome instead of censoring them; (ii) SOCX pension-heavy total spending vs the universal-services measure (health+edu) \u2014 the placebo arm; (iii) Houle's coup-era discrete survival vs graded erosion-era outcome; (iv) ANRR's 1960-2010 all-democratizations dividend vs the post-1990 wave; (v) H&K's case-based evidence vs the interaction design; and a MEASUREMENT-RISK section \u2014 PIP Gini 5-year waves/coverage in the young-democratizer sample, WHO GGHE-D pre-2000 gaps, WID interpolation, OWID vintage/anchor-year caveats \u2014 listing which items must go to follow_up_questions.\\n\\nPHASE 4 \u2014 WRITE OUTPUTS (2:50-3:00, 10 min)\\nresearch_report.md sections: 1) Executive summary (the five specs + key calibration numbers, <=1 page); 2) Per-source extraction dossier (paper -> specs with quotes + URLs); 3) Mechanism->specification mapping table; 4) Divergences & bridging decisions; 5) OWID availability audit; 6) Measurement risks and open items; 7) Full source list with URLs/DOIs (mark PAYWALLED-ABSTRACT-ONLY where applicable).\\nresearch_out.json: answer = <=800-word synthesis covering: what each study contributes, the five specs at specification level, the two must-bridge divergences, and the top-3 data risks; sources = every source actually used (title, url, doi, one-line contribution); follow_up_questions = actionable questions for iteration 2, including at minimum: (a) does OWID host V-Dem polarization and ILO educated-youth unemployment (and which proxy if not)?; (b) is Rau-Stokes replication data (episode list) public?; (c) PIP Gini coverage for post-1990 democratizers vs WID top-10 as treatment?; (d) WHO GGHE-D pre-2000 gaps \u2014 proxy with enrollment/immunization or later-start values?; (e) OECD SOCX composition confirmation for the placebo; (f) which regime classification (RoW vs BMR) defines the transition window?\\nValidate research_out.json with a JSON parser if available (python -m json.tool via the tooling venv); otherwise manual review.\\n\\nPHASE 5 \u2014 QA (final minutes, fold into Phase 4)\\n- No citation without a fetched source; no unexplained number; every number in the briefing has a URL+quote in notes.\\n- Each of the five specifications is grounded in >=1 dossier.\\n- Any item not extracted is explicitly marked UNVERIFIED in its dossier and mirrored into follow_up_questions.\\n- Outputs exist, JSON is valid, and the report is self-contained enough for iteration 2's experiment designer to implement specs 1-5 without re-reading the primary papers.\\n\\nFAILURE SCENARIOS & FALLBACKS\\n- Paywall (QJE, World Politics, ASR, CUP): NBER/author PDFs (Campante-Chor w16752, AES w17357, ANRR w19746, Houle's MSU page), Google Books preview for H&K, journal abstracts, and published reviews for case lists. Mark PAYWALLED-ABSTRACT-ONLY; extract what is available; never fabricate.\\n- PNAS SI inaccessible: work from the main text; note which tables came from the SI.\\n- Search engine failure / rate limits: switch to --mode scholarly (OpenAlex/Crossref); retry with backoff; if the ability server is down, report it rather than improvising.\\n- Time pressure: prioritize A (Rau-Stokes), B (ANRR dividend), E (H&K case list), D (Campante-Chor), then the OWID audit; trim G/H to abstracts; compress F to the mechanism statement.\\n- Conflicting metadata (e.g., ANRR venue): record both with URLs and flag for the experiment (the citation matters for the paper's references).\\n\\nCONSTRAINTS\\n- Zero LLM spend: this artifact needs NO OpenRouter calls (the $10 budget is untouched); all work uses free web tooling.\\n- All files written inside the executor's workspace (absolute paths, subdirectories notes/ OK).\\n- No dataset downloads, no data computation, no model estimation \u2014 this is pure information gathering + synthesis. The only scripts used are the web-tool search/fetch/grep helpers.\",\n  \"explanation\": \"Iteration 2's experiment can only adjudicate Rau-Stokes versus Haggard-Kaufman through the welfare gate if it faithfully reuses their exact measurement and design choices instead of reinventing them: the Rau-Stokes sample screen, erosion-episode coding, Gini source and lag, controls, model family and clustering determine whether the iteration-2 baseline main effect is comparable to the flagship finding; the ANRR event-study form, outcome list (top-decile share, public goods), and timing conventions size the welfare-dependent dividend test; Houle's survival setup justifies the first-democratic-decade framing; Campante-Chor's schooling-times-opportunities interaction is the template for the educated-youth-mismatch arm; Haggard-Kaufman's backslider/survivor case list enables the qualitative-quantitative bridge and a sensitivity analysis; Acemoglu-Egorov-Sonin's endogenous-limits-to-redistribution logic operationalizes the pre-commitment mechanism; and the policy-feedback/universalism evidence (Pierson, Campbell, Korpi-Palme, Manacorda et al., Dolls-Fuest-Peichl, CEQ incidence) supplies the micro-foundations for the constituency-formation and automatic-stabilization mechanisms as well as the cash-vs-universal-services corollary. The OWID availability audit additionally de-risks the hard constraint that everything be testable on OWID panels (LDI, PIP/WID inequality, GGHE-D and EdStats spending, SOCX placebo, polarization, ILO mismatch) before any experiment is written. The deliverable is a design briefing that maps every hypothesis mechanism to one of five concrete specifications \u2014 gate interaction, adjudication (main effect with and without interaction), erosion-onset event studies with lead-lag sequencing, the welfare-dependent dividend test, and the polarization/mismatch mechanism arms \u2014 and flags the divergences that must be bridged, most importantly the Rau-Stokes uninterrupted-democracy sample versus the young-democratizer sample and the pension-heavy SOCX composition versus the universal-services measure. Workflow per source: search (scholarly) -> fetch (gist) -> grep (exact equations, numbers, case lists); grep PDFs with ~200-300 char context. Parallelize aggressively (4-6 independent searches/fetches per turn); sequence only fetch-after-search; if the ability server is down, say so in the report. VERIFICATION RULES: every number, case, equation, quote must trace to a fetched source (URL + snippet in notes); never guess citations/DOIs - get them from Crossref/OpenAlex or the publisher page; if paywalled, mark PAYWALLED-ABSTRACT-ONLY, extract only what the abstract/Google Books/review gives, never invent details, and move missing items to follow_up_questions. || STEP 0 - DISCOVERY + SOURCE LOCK (0:00-0:15): run one scholarly search per target in parallel: (1) Rau Stokes income inequality erosion democracy PNAS; (2) Acemoglu Naidu Restrepo Robinson democracy redistribution inequality; (3) Houle inequality and democracy why inequality harms consolidation but not democratization; (4) Campante Chor why was the Arab world poised for revolution schooling economic opportunities; (5) Acemoglu Egorov Sonin a political theory of populism; (6) Haggard Kaufman backsliding democratic regress in the contemporary world; (7) Korpi Palme paradox of redistribution strategies of equality; (8) Pierson when effect becomes cause policy feedback and political change. Record in 00_sources.md a status column (FOUND/FETCHED/EXTRACTED/PAYWALLED-ABSTRACT-ONLY) plus a fetchable URL per item: PNAS article page + Supplementary PDF (pnas.*.sapp.pdf); NBER working-paper PDFs; author-hosted PDFs (Houle MSU faculty page, Chor faculty page, Acemoglu MIT page); publisher landing pages (OUP QJE, Cambridge World Politics, Sage JESP, Annual Reviews); Google Books preview for the Cambridge book. Also confirm two regime-data papers: Luehrmann-Tannenberg-Lindberg Regimes of the World (Democratization 2018) and Boix-Miller-Rosato A Complete Data Set of Political Regimes 1800-2007 (CPS 2013) - they define transition dates and the uninterrupted-democracy sample. NOTE: the hypothesis cites ANRR 2015 as AER; expected reality is a Handbook of Income Distribution Vol.2 (Elsevier 2015) chapter (NBER w19746 is the working paper) - VERIFY the true venue via Crossref/OpenAlex and record both citations. || STEP 1 - EXTRACTION DOSSIERS A-H (0:15-2:15): fetch main text, grep exact items, record into the numbered notes files. Priority when time is short: A, B, E, D load-bearing; trim G/H first; F can compress to mechanism statement. [A] Rau-Stokes 2025 PNAS (25 min): locate via PNAS DOI + SI PDF; grep patterns: uninterrupted, sample, erosion, episode, Gini|SWIID|PIP|inequality, hazard|Cox|logit|OLS|probit, control, cluster, lag, 1995|2000|2020, threshold; record: (a) exact sample definition - regime classification used to screen countries, which countries excluded (post-1990 democratizers below threshold?), N countries, N country-years, period; (b) full erosion-episode list and coding rule (binary onset? sustained LDI drop? threshold?); (c) inequality variable: source (SWIID/PIP/WID), transformation, lag structure; (d) full control set; (e) model family, fixed effects, clustering; (f) headline coefficients/SEs to calibrate the iter-2 main effect; (g) whether ANY interaction/moderation is run (expected none - that gap is the gate); (h) replication-data availability; spend <=5 min on published commentary/replication critiques. [B] ANRR 2015 dividend (30 min): NBER w19746 PDF + published landing page; grep: democratiz, event stud, top decile|top 10|income share, school|education, tax, public good, matching, 1960, equation, cluster, sample; record: (a) democratization-event definition + regime source; (b) sample N and years; (c) outcome list - top-decile income share (WID?), tax/GDP, social/education/health spending, schooling; (d) estimation design - OLS country+year FE, dynamic leads/lags event-study form, matching; write out the printed equations; (e) controls + clustering; (f) headline magnitudes at +10 years for the inequality and public-goods outcomes (sizes the welfare-dependent dividend test); (g) their caveats on the inequality leg; skim ANRR 2019 JPE Democracy Does Cause Growth (<=10 min) for event-study timing conventions only. [C] Houle 2009 World Politics (15 min): Cambridge Core + full-text PDF (MSU faculty page; Project MUSE fallback); grep: hazard|survival|Weibull|Cox, Gini, lag|five-year|moving average, control, sample, consolid, breakdown; record: (a) outcome + sample (democracies, years, N breakdowns); (b) inequality operationalization (source, interpolation, lagged moving average); (c) model family, controls, clustering; (d) key coefficients for the young-democracy consolidation frame; (e) his consolidation-as-survival definition (justifies the first-democratic-decade framing). [D] Campante-Chor 2012 QJE (20 min): NBER w16752 PDF or Chor faculty page; grep: interaction, schooling, opportunit, unemployment, instrument, probit|logit, Arab, coefficient, Figure|Table; record: (a) unit of analysis (country-level + individual Arab Barometer); (b) DV coding (protest onset/intensity); (c) the two interacted regressors and exact construction (schooling; economic-opportunities measure and its components); (d) the printed interaction equation and sign pattern (schooling raises protest only where opportunities poor); (e) endogeneity treatment (IV? lagged schooling growth?); (f) headline marginal effects - calibrates the iter-2 mismatch arm (ILO educated-youth unemployment). [E] Haggard-Kaufman 2021 (25 min): Cambridge book page + Google Books preview + their Journal of Democracy pieces + published reviews (Perspectives on Politics, APSR) + their 2016 Annual Review of Political Science Democratization During the Third Wave; grep: backslider, case, Hungary, Poland, Turkey, Venezuela, South Africa, inequality, grievance, polariz, did not|no evidence; record: (a) their operational definition of backsliding (index, threshold, period); (b) the complete backslider list AND the survivor comparison set - critical for iter-2 sensitivity analysis (does the gate hold within their classification?); (c) exact evidence for grievances/inequality did not differentiate (comparisons, tables, numbers) and their polarization measures (V-Dem v2x_polarization? own coding?); (d) their welfare-state material (2008 book Development Democracy and Welfare States; 2016 ARPS) on WHEN new democracies built welfare states - feeds the first-decade measurement design. [F] Acemoglu-Egorov-Sonin 2013 QJE (15 min): NBER w17357 PDF or Acemoglu MIT page; grep: populist, redistribut, inequality, Proposition, equilibrium, commit; record: (a) model skeleton (agents, candidates, timing, what the populist promises); (b) the endogenous-limits-to-redistribution result and why high inequality opens the populist equilibrium; (c) plain-English mechanism statement for mechanism (iii) pre-commitment; (d) comparative statics mappable to observable proxies (low pre-committed redistribution + high inequality -> polarization/populist support). [G] Policy feedback + universalism (20 min, trim to 10 if behind): fetch abstracts/full texts as available: Pierson 1993 When Effect Becomes Cause (policy feedback - programs create constituencies); Campbell 2012 ARPS Policy Makes Mass Politics; Korpi-Palme 1998 ASR Paradox of Redistribution (universalism reduces inequality more than targeting - justifies universal-services not cash-transfer gate); Skocpol 1991 Targeting within Universalism; Manacorda-Miguel-Vigorito 2011 AEJ:Applied PANES transfers -> incumbent support (cash buys incumbents not institutions); Zucco 2013 Bolsa Familia -> Lula vote; Dolls-Fuest-Peichl 2012 J.Public Econ automatic stabilizers absorb ~38% EU vs ~32% US of income shocks (quantifies mechanism i); CEQ Commitment to Equity benefit-incidence evidence that health+education spending reaches lower/middle deciles (mechanism ii); IMF Fiscal Monitor Oct 2017 on fiscal redistribution and the middle class. Record per item: one-line takeaway + citation + which gate mechanism (i)/(ii)/(iii) or the cash-vs-services corollary it supports. [H] Regime-data papers (10 min): RoW 2018 typology - what qualifies as entering electoral democracy, transition-year conventions; BMR 2013 - democracy definition used as sample screen, vintage/updates; V-Dem Backsliding in the World report only if instantly accessible. Record definitional thresholds + the rule for coding post-1990 democratizer (transition 1989-2005) and uninterrupted democracy. || STEP 2 - OWID AVAILABILITY AUDIT (2:15-2:30, 15 min): verify by fetching each OWID page (pages only - NO dataset downloads): series existence, source attribution, date coverage; record slug + caveats in 08_owid_audit.md: (a) liberal-democracy-index (V-Dem v2x_libdem); (b) electoral-democracy-index and Regimes of the World categories if hosted; (c) World Bank PIP Gini page + WID Gini page; (d) WID top-10-percent-share page; (e) domestic-health-expenditure-share-of-gdp (WHO GGHE-D) - CRITICAL: scrutinize start years (many countries lack pre-2000 values; first-decade end for 1989-95 transitions is 1999-2005); (f) government-expenditure-on-education-share-of-gdp (WB EdStats); (g) total-social-expenditure-gdp-oecd (OECD SOCX) - record metadata on composition (pensions share) for the placebo arm; (h) political-polarization (V-Dem v2x_polarization) - hosted?; (i) ILO education/mismatch series (unemployment by education level, educated youth) - hosted? If (h)/(i) missing, put into follow_up_questions with OWID proxy candidates (overall youth unemployment, school enrollment). || STEP 3 - SYNTHESIS: DESIGN BRIEFING (2:30-2:50, 20 min): distill into mechanism->specification mapping; the report MUST give, for each of the five specifications, the concrete form (variables, lags, fixed effects, clustering, sample, controls) and cite the dossier(s) each element comes from: (1) GATE INTERACTION (core): LDI_{c,t} ~ Gini_{c,t-5} + Welf_c0 + (Gini x Welf_c0) + controls, country+year FE, cluster by country; Welf_c0 = health+edu spending %GDP at first-decade end, time-invariant pre-determined; expectation: inequality effect ~0 in the high-welfare arm, strongly negative in the thin arm (dossier B dividend logic + dossier F commitment logic + dossier G universalism justification). (2) ADJUDICATION SPEC: same sample, first WITHOUT interaction (baseline main effect - compare to dossier A Rau-Stokes estimate), then WITH interaction; this is the empirical reconciliation (effect concentrated in the thin-welfare arm). (3) EROSION-ONSET EVENT STUDIES + LEAD-LAG SEQUENCING: dynamic coefficients around erosion onset (-5..+5) estimated separately by welfare arm; sequencing tests - inequality surge and public-service retrenchment LEAD LDI decline by 3-5 years in the thin arm only; no anticipatory welfare decline before erosion in the high arm; ground onset in dossier A episode list and dossier H sequencing; guard reverse causality with Szikra-Oektem 2023 JESP (welfare states transformed UNDER backsliding - quick search <=10 min or cite abstract). (4) WELFARE-DEPENDENT DIVIDEND TEST: dossier B ANRR event-study restricted to post-1990 democratizers, split by first-decade welfare legacy; outcome WID top-10% share (plus health+edu spending); then whether first-decade dividend size predicts 2010-2022 LDI resilience. (5) MECHANISM ARMS: (a) polarization - v2x_polarization as outcome of Gini x thin welfare (mediation to LDI decline), anchored in dossier E; (b) mismatch - Campante-Chor template (dossier D) transposed: schooling/education expansion x ILO educated-youth-unemployment x thin welfare on Delta-LDI, OWID availability from STEP 2. Plus DIVERGENCES-TO-BRIDGE: (i) Rau-Stokes uninterrupted-democracy sample 1995-2020 vs young democratizers 1989-2005 - iter-2 must KEEP below-threshold democratizers with continuous LDI instead of censoring; (ii) SOCX pension-heavy total spending vs universal-services measure (health+edu) - the placebo arm; (iii) Houle coup-era discrete survival vs graded erosion-era outcome; (iv) ANRR 1960-2010 all-democratizations dividend vs post-1990 wave; (v) H&K case-based evidence vs the interaction design. Plus MEASUREMENT-RISK section: PIP Gini 5-year waves/coverage in the young-democratizer sample, WHO GGHE-D pre-2000 gaps, WID interpolation, OWID vintage/anchor caveats - list which items go to follow_up_questions. || STEP 4 - WRITE OUTPUTS (2:50-3:00, 10 min): research_report.md sections: 1) Executive summary (five specs + key calibration numbers, <=1 page); 2) Per-source extraction dossier with quotes + URLs; 3) Mechanism->specification mapping table; 4) Divergences and bridging decisions; 5) OWID availability audit; 6) Measurement risks and open items; 7) Full source list with URLs/DOIs (mark PAYWALLED-ABSTRACT-ONLY). research_out.json: answer <=800 words (what each study contributes; the five specs at specification level; the two must-bridge divergences; top-3 data risks); sources = every source used (title, url, doi, one-line contribution); follow_up_questions at minimum: (a) does OWID host V-Dem polarization and ILO educated-youth unemployment (which proxy if not)?; (b) is Rau-Stokes replication data (episode list) public?; (c) PIP Gini coverage for post-1990 democratizers vs WID top-10 as treatment?; (d) WHO GGHE-D pre-2000 gaps - proxy with enrollment/immunization or later-start values?; (e) OECD SOCX composition confirmation for the placebo; (f) which regime classification (RoW vs BMR) defines the transition window? Validate research_out.json with a JSON parser if available (python -m json.tool via the tooling venv); otherwise manual review. || STEP 5 - QA (fold into STEP 4): no citation without a fetched source; no unexplained number - every number has a URL+quote in notes; each of the five specifications grounded in >=1 dossier; any unextracted item marked UNVERIFIED in its dossier and mirrored into follow_up_questions; outputs exist, JSON valid, report self-contained so iteration 2 implements specs 1-5 without re-reading the primary papers. || FAILURE SCENARIOS AND FALLBACKS: paywall (QJE, World Politics, ASR, Cambridge) - use NBER/author PDFs (w16752, w17357, w19746, Houle MSU page), Google Books preview for H&K, journal abstracts, published reviews for case lists; mark PAYWALLED-ABSTRACT-ONLY; never fabricate.
