RESEARCH_PLAN NEW (final, ASCII, single logical line — I'll put it on ONE line in the file):

"RESEARCH EXECUTOR ROLE || DELIVERABLES: (1) research_report.md - the DESIGN BRIEFING (main deliverable, sections per STEP 4); (2) research_out.json with answer, sources, follow_up_questions; (3) per-source notes under notes/ (00_sources.md, 01_rau_stokes.md, 02_anrr.md, 03_houle.md, 04_campante_chor.md, 05_haggard_kaufman.md, 06_aes_populism.md, 07_policy_feedback.md, 08_owid_audit.md, 09_datasets.md) so nothing is lost on timeout. || TOOLING: read the aii-web-tools skill first; prefer built-in WebSearch/WebFetch (load once via ToolSearch select:WebSearch,WebFetch if deferred); otherwise use aii_fast_web_search.py --query <q> --mode scholarly (OpenAlex/Crossref for DOIs) or --mode general (discovery), and aii_fast_web_fetch.py fetch --url <u> --max-chars N and grep --url <u> --pattern <regex> -i for exact text and numbers in long pages and PDFs. Workflow per source: search (scholarly) -> fetch (gist) -> grep (exact equations, numbers, case lists); grep PDFs with 200-300 char context; parallelize aggressively (4-6 independent searches or fetches per turn) and sequence only fetch-after-search; if the ability server is down, say so in the report and do not improvise another search method. VERIFICATION RULES: every number, case, equation and quote must trace to a fetched source (URL plus snippet in notes); never guess citations or DOIs - get them from Crossref/OpenAlex or the publisher page; if a source is paywalled, mark it PAYWALLED-ABSTRACT-ONLY, extract only what the abstract/Google Books/review gives, never invent details, and move missing items to follow_up_questions. || STEP 0 - SOURCE LOCK (0:00-0:15): run one scholarly-mode search per target in parallel: (1) Rau Stokes income inequality erosion democracy PNAS; (2) Acemoglu Naidu Restrepo Robinson democracy redistribution inequality; (3) Houle inequality and democracy why inequality harms consolidation but not democratization; (4) Campante Chor why was the Arab world poised for revolution schooling economic opportunities; (5) Acemoglu Egorov Sonin a political theory of populism; (6) Haggard Kaufman backsliding democratic regress in the contemporary world; (7) Korpi Palme paradox of redistribution strategies of equality; (8) Pierson when effect becomes cause policy feedback and political change. Record in 00_sources.md a status column (FOUND, FETCHED, EXTRACTED, PAYWALLED-ABSTRACT-ONLY) plus one fetchable URL per item: PNAS article page and its Supplementary PDF (pnas.*.sapp.pdf); NBER working-paper PDFs; author-hosted PDFs (Houle MSU faculty page, Chor faculty page, Acemoglu MIT page); publisher landing pages (OUP QJE, Cambridge World Politics, Sage JESP, Annual Reviews); Google Books preview for the Cambridge book. Also confirm two regime-data papers: Luehrmann-Tannenberg-Lindberg Regimes of the World (Democratization 2018) and Boix-Miller-Rosato A Complete Data Set of Political Regimes 1800-2007 (CPS 2013) - they define transition dates and the uninterrupted-democracy sample the experiment inherits. NOTE: the hypothesis cites ANRR 2015 as AER but the expected reality is a Handbook of Income Distribution Vol.2 (Elsevier 2015) chapter (NBER w19746 is the working paper); VERIFY the true venue via Crossref/OpenAlex and record both citations. || STEP 1 - EXTRACTION DOSSIERS A-H (0:15-2:15): for each: fetch main text, grep exact items, record into the numbered notes file. Priority when time is short: A, B, E, D are load-bearing; trim G/H first; F can compress to a mechanism statement. [A] Rau and Stokes 2025 PNAS (25 min): locate via PNAS DOI plus SI PDF; grep patterns: uninterrupted, sample, erosion, episode, Gini|SWIID|PIP|inequality, hazard|Cox|logit|OLS|probit, control, cluster, lag, 1995|2000|2020, threshold; record: (a) exact sample definition - which regime classification screens countries, which countries are excluded (post-1990 democratizers that fell below the democracy threshold?), N countries, N country-years, period; (b) the full erosion-episode list and coding rule (binary onset? sustained V-Dem LDI drop? threshold?); (c) inequality variable: exact source (SWIID/PIP/WID), transformation, lag structure; (d) full control set; (e) model family, fixed effects, clustering; (f) headline coefficients and SEs (calibrates the iter-2 main-effect magnitude); (g) whether they run ANY moderation or interaction analysis (expected none - that gap is the gate); (h) replication-data availability; spend at most 5 min on published commentary or replication critiques. [B] ANRR 2015 dividend design (30 min): NBER w19746 PDF plus published-version landing page; grep: democratiz, event stud, top decile|top 10|income share, school|education, tax, public good, matching, 1960, equation, cluster, sample; record: (a) democratization-event definition and regime-classification source; (b) sample N countries and years; (c) the full outcome list - top-decile income share (WID?), tax/GDP share, social/education/health spending, schooling; (d) estimation design - OLS with country and year FE, dynamic leads/lags event-study form, any matching estimator; write out their printed equation(s); (e) controls and clustering; (f) headline magnitudes: effect of democratization on the top-decile share and public-goods outcomes at +10 years (sizes the iter-2 welfare-dependent dividend test); (g) their own caveats on the inequality leg (the exact leg the hypothesis claims is welfare-gated); skim ANRR 2019 JPE Democracy Does Cause Growth (at most 10 min) only for event-study window and timing conventions. [C] Houle 2009 World Politics (15 min): Cambridge Core landing page plus full-text PDF (MSU faculty page; Project MUSE as fallback); grep: hazard|survival|Weibull|Cox, Gini, lag|five-year|moving average, control, sample, consolid, breakdown; record: (a) outcome and sample (democracies, years, N regime breakdowns); (b) inequality operationalization (source, interpolation, lagged moving average); (c) model family, controls, clustering; (d) key coefficients for the young-democracy consolidation frame; (e) his operational definition of consolidation-as-survival (justifies the first-democratic-decade framing). [D] Campante and Chor 2012 QJE (20 min): NBER w16752 PDF or Chor faculty page; grep: interaction, schooling, opportunit, unemployment, instrument, probit|logit, Arab, coefficient, Figure|Table; record: (a) unit of analysis (country-level and individual Arab Barometer); (b) DV coding (protest onset or intensity); (c) the two interacted regressors and their exact construction - schooling and the economic-opportunities measure with its components; (d) the printed interaction equation and its sign pattern (schooling raises protest only where opportunities are poor); (e) endogeneity treatment (IV? lagged schooling growth?); (f) headline marginal effects - calibrates the iter-2 mismatch arm (ILO educated-youth unemployment). [E] Haggard and Kaufman 2021 (25 min): Cambridge book page plus Google Books preview, their Journal of Democracy pieces, published reviews (Perspectives on Politics, APSR) that summarize the case list, and their 2016 Annual Review of Political Science Democratization During the Third Wave; grep: backslider, case, Hungary, Poland, Turkey, Venezuela, South Africa, inequality, grievance, polariz, did not|no evidence; record: (a) their operational definition of backsliding (index, threshold, period); (b) the complete backslider list AND the survivor comparison set - critical for iter-2 sensitivity analysis (does the gate hold within their own classification?); (c) the exact evidence for the claim that grievances and inequality did not differentiate outcomes (tables or reported numbers) and their polarization measures (V-Dem v2x_polarization or their own coding); (d) their welfare-state material (2008 book Development Democracy and Welfare States; 2016 ARPS) on WHEN new democracies built welfare states - feeds the first-decade measurement design. [F] Acemoglu Egorov Sonin 2013 QJE (15 min): NBER w17357 PDF or Acemoglu MIT page; grep: populist, redistribut, inequality, Proposition, equilibrium, commit; record: (a) model skeleton (agents, candidates, timing, what the populist promises); (b) the endogenous-limits-to-redistribution result - why high inequality makes elites limit redistribution and opens the populist equilibrium; (c) a plain-English mechanism statement for mechanism (iii) pre-commitment of the gate; (d) comparative statics mappable to observable proxies (low pre-committed redistribution plus high inequality implies polarization and populist support). [G] Policy feedback and universalism micro-evidence (20 min, trim to 10 if behind): fetch abstracts or full texts as available: Pierson 1993 World Politics When Effect Becomes Cause (programs create constituencies); Campbell 2012 Annual Review of Political Science Policy Makes Mass Politics; Korpi and Palme 1998 ASR The Paradox of Redistribution (universalism reduces inequality more than targeting - justifies a universal-services not a cash-transfer gate measure); Skocpol 1991 Targeting within Universalism; Manacorda Miguel Vigorito 2011 AEJ Applied PANES transfers and incumbent support (cash buys incumbents, not institutions); Zucco 2013 Bolsa Familia and Lula vote; Dolls Fuest Peichl 2012 J Public Economics automatic stabilizers absorb roughly 38 percent of income shocks in the EU and 32 percent in the US (quantifies mechanism i); CEQ Commitment to Equity benefit-incidence evidence that health and education spending reaches lower and middle deciles of the distribution in developing countries (mechanism ii); IMF Fiscal Monitor October 2017 on fiscal redistribution and the middle class. Record per item: one-line takeaway, citation, and which gate mechanism (i) automatic stabilization, (ii) constituency formation, (iii) pre-commitment or the cash-versus-services corollary it supports. [H] Regime-data papers (10 min): RoW 2018 - typology, what qualifies as entering electoral democracy, transition-year conventions; BMR 2013 - democracy definition used as the sample screen, data vintage; V-Dem Backsliding in the World report only if instantly accessible. Record definitional thresholds plus the rule for coding a post-1990 democratizer (transition 1989-2005) and an uninterrupted democracy. || STEP 2 - OWID AVAILABILITY AUDIT (2:15-2:30, 15 min): verify by fetching each OWID page (pages only - NO dataset downloads); record the series slug, source attribution, date coverage, and caveats in 08_owid_audit.md: (a) liberal-democracy-index (V-Dem v2x_libdem); (b) electoral-democracy-index and Regimes of the World categories if hosted; (c) World Bank PIP Gini page and the WID Gini page; (d) WID top-10-percent income-share page; (e) domestic-health-expenditure-share-of-gdp (WHO GGHE-D) - CRITICAL: scrutinize start years; many countries have no pre-2000 values, and the first-decade end for 1989-95 transitions is 1999-2005; (f) government-expenditure-on-education-share-of-gdp (WB EdStats); (g) total-social-expenditure-gdp-oecd (OECD SOCX) - record any metadata on composition (pensions share) for the placebo arm; (h) political-polarization (V-Dem v2x_polarization) - hosted?; (i) ILO education and mismatch series (unemployment by education level, educated youth) - hosted? If (h) or (i) is absent, move it to follow_up_questions with OWID proxy candidates (overall youth unemployment, school enrollment). || STEP 3 - SYNTHESIS: THE DESIGN BRIEFING (2:30-2:50, 20 min): distill everything into the mechanism-to-specification mapping. The report MUST give, for each of the five specifications, the concrete form (variables, lags, fixed effects, clustering, sample, controls) and cite which dossier each element comes from: (1) GATE INTERACTION (core): LDI_{c,t} ~ Gini_{c,t-5} + Welf_c0 + (Gini x Welf_c0) + controls, country and year fixed effects, cluster by country; Welf_c0 = health plus education spending as percent of GDP measured at the end of the first democratic decade, time-invariant and pre-determined; expectation: the inequality effect is near zero in the high-welfare arm and strongly negative in the thin arm (dossier B dividend logic plus dossier F commitment logic as theory, dossier G universalism as justification). (2) ADJUDICATION SPEC: same sample, first WITHOUT the interaction (baseline main effect - compare sign and magnitude with dossier A Rau-Stokes estimate), then WITH the interaction; this is the empirical reconciliation of the two literatures (effect concentrated in the thin-welfare arm). (3) EROSION-ONSET EVENT STUDIES AND LEAD-LAG SEQUENCING: dynamic coefficients around erosion onset (-5 to +5) estimated separately by welfare arm; sequencing tests - inequality surges and public-service retrenchment LEAD LDI declines by 3-5 years in the thin arm only, and NO anticipatory welfare decline before erosion onset in the high arm; ground the onset coding in dossier A episode list and dossier H sequencing; guard reverse causality with Szikra and Oektem 2023 JESP (welfare states are transformed UNDER backsliding; quick search at most 10 min or cite from abstract). (4) WELFARE-DEPENDENT DIVIDEND TEST: dossier B ANRR event-study design restricted to post-1990 democratizers, split by first-decade welfare legacy; outcome: WID top-10 percent share (plus health and education spending); then test whether first-decade dividend size predicts 2010-2022 LDI resilience. (5) MECHANISM ARMS: (a) polarization - v2x_polarization as outcome of Gini x thin-welfare (mediation toward LDI decline), anchored in dossier E; (b) mismatch - Campante-Chor interaction template (dossier D) transposed: schooling and education expansion x ILO educated-youth unemployment x thin welfare on Delta-LDI, with OWID availability from STEP 2. Also produce a DIVERGENCES-TO-BRIDGE section: (i) Rau-Stokes uninterrupted-democracy sample 1995-2020 versus young democratizers 1989-2005 - iter-2 must KEEP below-threshold democratizers with the continuous LDI outcome instead of censoring them; (ii) SOCX pension-heavy total social spending versus the universal-services measure (health plus education) - the placebo arm; (iii) Houle coup-era discrete survival versus the graded erosion-era outcome; (iv) ANRR 1960-2010 all-democratizations dividend versus the post-1990 wave; (v) Haggard-Kaufman case-based evidence versus the interaction design. Plus a MEASUREMENT-RISK section: PIP Gini 5-year waves and coverage in the young-democratizer sample, WHO GGHE-D pre-2000 gaps, WID interpolation, OWID vintage and anchor-year caveats - listing which items must go to follow_up_questions. || STEP 4 - WRITE OUTPUTS (2:50-3:00, 10 min): research_report.md sections: 1) Executive summary (the five specs and key calibration numbers, at most one page); 2) Per-source extraction dossier with quotes and URLs; 3) Mechanism-to-specification mapping table; 4) Divergences and bridging decisions; 5) OWID availability audit; 6) Measurement risks and open items; 7) Full source list with URLs and DOIs (mark PAYWALLED-ABSTRACT-ONLY where applicable). research_out.json: answer at most 800 words covering what each study contributes, the five specs at specification level, the two must-bridge divergences, and the top-3 data risks; sources - every source actually used (title, url, doi, one-line contribution); follow_up_questions - at minimum: (a) does OWID host V-Dem polarization and ILO educated-youth unemployment (and which proxy if not)?; (b) is the Rau-Stokes replication data (episode list) public?; (c) PIP Gini coverage for post-1990 democratizers versus WID top-10 as the treatment?; (d) WHO GGHE-D pre-2000 gaps - proxy with enrollment and immunization or use later-start values?; (e) OECD SOCX composition confirmation for the placebo; (f) which regime classification (RoW versus BMR) defines the transition window? Validate research_out.json with a JSON parser if available (python -m json.tool via the tooling venv); otherwise manual review. || STEP 5 - QA (fold into STEP 4): no citation without a fetched source; no unexplained number - every number in the briefing has a URL plus quote in notes; each of the five specifications grounded in at least one dossier; any item not extracted is explicitly marked UNVERIFIED in its dossier and mirrored into follow_up_questions; outputs exist, JSON is valid, and the report is self-contained enough that iteration 2 implements specs 1-5 without re-reading the primary papers. || FAILURE SCENARIOS AND FALLBACKS: paywall (QJE, World Politics, ASR, Cambridge) - use NBER or author PDFs (w16752, w17357, w19746, Houle MSU page), Google Books preview for Haggard-Kaufman, journal abstracts, and published reviews for case lists; mark PAYWALLED-ABSTRACT-ONLY and never fabricate. PNAS SI inaccessible - work from the main text and note which tables came from the SI. Search failure or rate limits - switch to --mode scholarly (OpenAlex/Crossref), retry with backoff; if the ability server is down, report it rather than improvising. Time pressure - prioritize A (Rau-Stokes), B (ANRR), E (Haggard-Kaufman), D (Campante-Chor), then the OWID audit; trim G/H to abstracts; compress F to the mechanism statement. Conflicting metadata (for example the ANRR venue) - record both versions with URLs and flag for the experiment. || CONSTRAINTS: zero LLM spend - no OpenRouter calls needed (the 10 USD budget stays untouched); all files written under the executor workspace with absolute paths (notes/ subdirectory allowed); no dataset downloads, no data computation, no model estimation - pure information gathering and synthesis; the only scripts used are the web-tool search, fetch and grep helpers."

EXPLANATION NEW (ASCII):
"Iteration 2's experiment can only adjudicate Rau-Stokes versus Haggard-Kaufman through the welfare gate if it faithfully reuses their exact measurement and design choices instead of reinventing them: the Rau-Stokes sample screen, erosion-episode coding, Gini source and lag, controls, model family and clustering determine whether the iteration-2 baseline main effect is comparable to the flagship finding; the ANRR event-study form, outcome list (top-decile share, public goods) and timing conventions size the welfare-dependent dividend test; Houle's survival setup justifies the first-democratic-decade framing; Campante-Chor's schooling-times-opportunities interaction is the template for the educated-youth-mismatch arm; Haggard-Kaufman's backslider and survivor case list enables the qualitative-quantitative bridge and a sensitivity analysis; Acemoglu-Egorov-Sonin's endogenous-limits-to-redistribution logic operationalizes the pre-commitment mechanism; and the policy-feedback and universalism evidence (Pierson, Campbell, Korpi-Palme, Manacorda et al., Dolls-Fuest-Peichl, CEQ incidence) supplies the micro-foundations for the constituency-formation and automatic-stabilization mechanisms and the cash-versus-universal-services corollary. The OWID availability audit additionally de-risks the hard constraint that everything be testable on OWID panels (LDI, PIP/WID inequality, GGHE-D and EdStats spending, SOCX placebo, polarization, ILO mismatch) before any experiment is written. The deliverable is a design briefing that maps every hypothesis mechanism to one of five concrete specifications - gate interaction, adjudication (main effect with and without interaction), erosion-onset event studies with lead-lag sequencing, the welfare-dependent dividend test, and the polarization and mismatch mechanism arms - and flags the divergences that must be bridged, most importantly the Rau-Stokes uninterrupted-democracy sample versus the young-democratizer sample and the pension-heavy SOCX composition versus the universal-services measure. Without this briefing, the experiment would risk either replicating a main effect everyone already knows or testing a gate whose operationalizations no longer connect to the literature it claims to reconcile."

STEP-BY-STEP RESEARCH PLAN (3 h budget; pure web research; zero LLM spend — no OpenRouter calls required for this artifact; everything free via web tooling).\n\nROLE & DELIVERABLES\nYou are the RESEARCH executor. Produce exactly two outputs inside your workspace:\n1. research_report.md — the DESIGN BRIEFING (main deliverable; structure in Phase 4).\n2. research_out.json — {\"answer\": <concise synthesis>, \"sources\": [{\"title\",\"url\",\"doi\",\"contribution\"}], \"follow_up_questions\": [...]}.\nSave per-source extraction notes under notes/ as you go (notes/01_rau_stokes.md, 02_anrr.md, 03_houle.md, 04_campante_chor.md, 05_haggard_kaufman.md, 06_aes_populism.md, 07_policy_feedback.md, 08_owid_audit.md, 09_datasets.md, 00_sources.md) so nothing is lost on a timeout.\n\nTOOLING (read the aii-web-tools skill first)\n- Use built-in WebSearch/WebFetch if available (load once via ToolSearch(\"select:WebSearch,WebFetch\") if they appear as deferred tools). Otherwise use the skill scripts: aii_fast_web_search.py --query \"...\" --mode scholarly (OpenAlex/Crossref for DOIs and reliable metadata) and --mode general (discovery); aii_fast_web_fetch.py fetch --url ... --max-chars N and grep --url ... --pattern \"regex\" -i for exact text/numbers in long pages and PDFs.\n- Workflow per source: search (scholarly) -> fetch (gist) -> grep (exact equations, numbers, case lists). Grep PDFs with context windows (~200-300 chars).\n- Parallelize aggressively: launch 4-6 independent searches/fetches per turn; only sequence fetch-after-search. If the ability server is down, say so in the report rather than improvising.\n- VERIFICATION RULE: every number, case, equation, and quote you record must trace to a fetched source (URL + snippet in your notes). Never guess citations or DOIs — get them from Crossref/OpenAlex or the publisher page. If a source is paywalled, mark it PAYWALLED-ABSTRACT-ONLY, extract what the abstract/Google Books/review gives, do NOT invent details, and route the missing items to follow_up_questions.\n\nPHASE 0 — DISCOVERY & SOURCE LOCK (0:00-0:15)\nRun one scholarly-mode search per target, in parallel, to lock exact titles/authors/venues/years/DOIs/URLs:\n1. \"Rau Stokes income inequality erosion democracy PNAS\"\n2. \"Acemoglu Naidu Restrepo Robinson democracy redistribution inequality\"\n3. \"Houle inequality and democracy why inequality harms consolidation but not democratization\"\n4. \"Campante Chor why was the Arab world poised for revolution schooling economic opportunities\"\n5. \"Acemoglu Egorov Sonin a political theory of populism\"\n6. \"Haggard Kaufman backsliding democratic regress in the contemporary world\"\n7. \"Korpi Palme paradox of redistribution strategies of equality\"\n8. \"Pierson when effect becomes cause policy feedback and political change\"\nFor each: record in notes/00_sources.md a status column (FOUND / FETCHED / EXTRACTED / PAYWALLED-ABSTRACT-ONLY) plus a fetchable URL: PNAS article page + its Supplementary PDF (pnas.*.sapp.pdf); NBER working-paper PDFs; author-hosted PDFs (Houle's MSU faculty page, Chor's faculty page, Acemoglu's MIT page); publisher landing pages (OUP QJE, Cambridge WP, SAGE JESP, Annual Reviews); Google Books preview for the CUP book. Also confirm two supporting dataset papers: Luehrmann-Tannenberg-Lindberg \"Regimes of the World\" (Democratization, 2018) and Boix-Miller-Rosato \"A Complete Data Set of Political Regimes, 1800-2007\" (CPS, 2013) — these define transition dates and the \"uninterrupted democracy\" sample the experiment inherits.\nNOTE: the hypothesis cites ANRR 2015 as \"AER\"; expected reality is a Handbook of Income Distribution Vol. 2 (Elsevier, 2015) chapter (NBER w19746 is the working paper). VERIFY the true venue via Crossref/OpenAlex and record both citations.\n\nPHASE 1 — EXTRACTION DOSSIERS (0:15-2:15, ~120 min)\nPer workstream: fetch main text -> grep exact items -> record into the numbered notes file. Priorities when short on time: A, B, E, D are load-bearing; trim G/H first; F can compress to mechanism statement.\n\nA. Rau & Stokes (2025, PNAS) — the flagship inequality->erosion main effect [25 min]\nLocate via PNAS DOI search + SI PDF. Grep patterns: \"uninterrupted\", \"sample\", \"erosion\", \"episode\", \"Gini|SWIID|PIP|inequality\", \"hazard|Cox|logit|OLS|probit\", \"control\", \"cluster\", \"lag\", \"1995|2000|2020\", \"threshold\".\nRecord: (a) exact sample definition — which regime classification screens countries, which countries are excluded (post-1990 democratizers that fell below the democracy threshold?), N countries, N country-years, period; (b) the full erosion-episode list and coding rule (binary onset? sustained V-Dem LDI drop? threshold?); (c) inequality variable: exact source (SWIID/PIP/WID), transformation, lag structure; (d) full control set; (e) model family, fixed effects, clustering; (f) headline coefficients/SEs (needed to calibrate the iter-2 main-effect magnitude); (g) whether they run ANY moderation/interaction analysis (expected: none — that gap is the gate); (h) mention of replication-data availability. Also spend <=5 min searching for published commentary/replication critiques of the paper.\n\nB. Acemoglu, Naidu, Restrepo & Robinson (2015) — the democratization-dividend design [30 min]\nLocate NBER w19746 PDF (free) + published-version landing page (ScienceDirect Handbook chapter; verify venue per Phase 0 note). Grep patterns: \"democratiz\", \"event stud\", \"top decile|top 10|income share\", \"school|education\", \"tax\", \"public good\", \"matching\", \"1960\", \"equation\", \"cluster\", \"sample\".\nRecord: (a) democratization-event definition and regime-classification source; (b) sample N countries and years; (c) full outcome list — top-decile income share (from WID?), tax/GDP share, social/education/health spending, schooling; (d) estimation design: OLS with country+year FE, dynamic leads/lags event-study form, any matching estimator — write out their actual equation(s) as printed; (e) controls and clustering; (f) headline magnitudes: effect of democratization on top-decile share at +10 years, on public-goods measures (these size the iter-2 welfare-dependent dividend test); (g) their own caveats about the inequality leg (the exact leg the hypothesis claims is welfare-gated). Skim ANRR 2019 JPE \"Democracy Does Cause Growth\" (<=10 min) only for event-study window/timing conventions.\n\nC. Houle (2009, World Politics) — survival/consolidation setup [15 min]\nLocate Cambridge Core landing page + full-text PDF (search \"Houle inequality democracy world politics 2009 pdf\"; MSU faculty page hosts it; Project MUSE as fallback for abstract + first pages). Grep: \"hazard|survival|Weibull|Cox\", \"Gini\", \"lag|five-year|moving average\", \"control\", \"sample\", \"consolid\", \"breakdown\".\nRecord: (a) outcome and sample (democracies, years, N regime breakdowns); (b) inequality operationalization (source: Deininger-Squire/WIID? interpolation? lagged moving average?); (c) model family (discrete-time? Cox? duration dependence?), controls, clustering; (d) key coefficients and interpretation for the young-democracy \"consolidation\" frame; (e) his operational definition of consolidation-as-survival (justifies the \"first democratic decade\" framing).\n\nD. Campante & Chor (2012, QJE) — the schooling x opportunities interaction template [20 min]\nLocate NBER w16752 PDF (free) or Chor's faculty page; OUP QJE page for the final version. Grep: \"interaction\", \"schooling\", \"opportunit\", \"unemployment\", \"instrument\", \"probit|logit\", \"Arab\", \"coefficient\", \"Figure|Table\".\nRecord: (a) unit of analysis (country-level and individual Arab Barometer); (b) DV coding (protest onset/intensity); (c) the two interacted regressors and their exact construction — schooling (average years / expansion) and the \"economic opportunities\" measure (components: educated-youth unemployment? income? corruption?); (d) the printed interaction equation and its sign pattern (schooling raises protest only where opportunities are poor); (e) endogeneity treatment (IV? lagged schooling growth?); (f) headline marginal effects — this calibrates the iter-2 mismatch arm (ILO educated-youth unemployment on OWID).\n\nE. Haggard & Kaufman (2021) — the coalitional null + case list [25 min]\nLocate CUP book page + Google Books preview (search inside for the intro/ch.1 case summary); their Journal of Democracy pieces (\"Backsliding: Democratic Regress in the Contemporary World\", JD 2021; verify existence/title); published reviews (Perspectives on Politics, APSR) that summarize the case list; their 2016 Annual Review of Political Science \"Democratization During the Third Wave\" for welfare-state development in new democracies. Grep: \"backslider\", \"case\", \"Hungary\", \"Poland\", \"Turkey\", \"Venezuela\", \"South Africa\", \"inequality\", \"grievance\", \"polariz\", \"did not|no evidence\".\nRecord: (a) their operational definition of backsliding (index, threshold, period); (b) the complete backslider list AND the survivors they compare against — critical for iter-2 sensitivity analysis (does the gate hold within H&K's own classification?); (c) the exact evidence for \"grievances/inequality did not differentiate\": inequality/crisis comparisons between backsliders and survivors (tables or reported numbers), and their polarization measures (V-Dem v2x_polarization? their own coding?); (d) their welfare-state material (2008 book \"Development, Democracy, and Welfare States\"; 2016 ARPS) on WHEN new democracies built welfare states — feeds the first-decade measurement design.\n\nF. Acemoglu, Egorov & Sonin (2013, QJE) — the pre-commitment mechanism [15 min]\nLocate NBER w17357 PDF (free) or Acemoglu's MIT page. Grep: \"populist\", \"redistribut\", \"inequality\", \"Proposition\", \"equilibrium\", \"commit\".\nRecord: (a) model skeleton: agents, candidates (established elite vs populist), timing, what the populist promises; (b) the \"endogenous limits to redistribution\" result — why high inequality makes elites limit redistribution and how that opens the populist equilibrium; (c) a plain-English mechanism statement usable in the paper (mechanism iii of the gate); (d) comparative statics that map to observable proxies (low pre-committed redistribution + high inequality => polarization/populist support).\n\nG. Policy feedback + universalism micro-evidence [20 min; trim to 10 if behind]\nFetch abstracts/full texts as available: Pierson (1993, World Politics) \"When Effect Becomes Cause\" (policy feedback: programs create constituencies); Campbell (2012, Annual Review of Political Science) \"Policy Makes Mass Politics\"; Korpi & Palme (1998, ASR) \"The Paradox of Redistribution\" (universalism reduces inequality more than targeting — the result justifying a universal-services, not CCT, gate measure); Skocpol (1991) \"Targeting within Universalism\"; Manacorda, Miguel & Vigorito (2011, AEJ: Applied) PANES transfers -> incumbent support (cash transfers buy incumbents, not institutions); Zucco (2013) Bolsa Familia -> Lula vote; Dolls, Fuest & Peichl (2012, J. Public Economics) automatic stabilizers absorb ~38% (EU) vs ~32% (US) of income shocks (quantifies mechanism i); CEQ / \"Commitment to Equity\" (Lustig et al.) benefit-incidence evidence that health+education spending reaches lower/middle deciles in developing countries (mechanism ii); IMF Fiscal Monitor (Oct 2017) on fiscal redistribution and the middle class. Record per item: one-line takeaway + citation + which gate mechanism (i)/(ii)/(iii) or the Cash-vs-Services corollary it supports.\n\nH. Supporting regime-data papers [10 min]\nLuehrmann-Tannenberg-Lindberg (2018, Democratization): RoW typology, what qualifies as entering \"electoral democracy\", transition-year conventions. Boix-Miller-Rosato (2013, CPS): democracy definition used as sample screen; note vintage/updates. V-Dem \"Backsliding in the World\" report only if instantly accessible (2020-2023 episode context). Record: definitional thresholds + the rule for coding \"post-1990 democratizer\" (transition 1989-2005) and \"uninterrupted democracy\".\n\nPHASE 2 — OWID AVAILABILITY AUDIT (2:15-2:30, 15 min)\nVerify by fetching each OWID page (pages only, NO dataset downloads): series exists, its source attribution, date coverage; record slug + caveats in notes/08_owid_audit.md:\n(a) liberal-democracy-index (V-Dem v2x_libdem); (b) electoral-democracy-index and Regimes-of-the-World categories if hosted; (c) World Bank PIP Gini page and the WID Gini page; (d) WID top-10% share page; (e) domestic-health-expenditure-share-gdp (WHO GGHE-D) — CRITICAL: scrutinize start years (many countries have no pre-2000 values; first-decade end for 1989-95 transitions is 1999-2005); (f) government-expenditure-on-education-share-of-gdp (WB EdStats); (g) total-social-expenditure-gdp-oecd (OECD SOCX) — record any metadata on composition (pensions share) for the placebo arm; (h) political-polarization page (V-Dem v2x_polarization) — hosted?; (i) ILO education/mismatch series (unemployment by education level, especially educated youth) — hosted? If (h)/(i) are absent, that goes into follow_up_questions with OWID proxy candidates (overall youth unemployment, school enrollment).\n\nPHASE 3 — SYNTHESIS: THE DESIGN BRIEFING (2:30-2:50, 20 min)\nDistill everything into the mechanism->specification mapping iteration 2 will implement. The report MUST specify, for each of the five specifications, the concrete form — variables, lags, fixed effects, clustering, sample, controls — and cite the source(s) each element comes from:\n1. GATE INTERACTION (core): e.g., LDI_{c,t} ~ Gini_{c,t-5} + Welf_c0 + (Gini x Welf_c0) + controls, country+year FE, cluster by country; Welf_c0 = health+edu spending %GDP at first-decade end, time-invariant (pre-determined). Theory expectation: inequality effect ~0 in the high-welfare arm, strongly negative in the thin arm (use dossier B's dividend logic + dossier F's commitment logic as the theoretical underpinning; dossier G for the universalism justification).\n2. ADJUDICATION SPEC: same sample, first WITHOUT interaction (baseline main effect — compare sign/magnitude to dossier A's Rau-Stokes estimate), then WITH interaction. This is the empirical reconciliation of Rau-Stokes (non-zero main effect) vs Haggard-Kaufman (no systematic economic grievance): the effect is concentrated in the thin-welfare arm.\n3. EROSION-ONSET EVENT STUDIES + LEAD-LAG SEQUENCING: dynamic coefficients around erosion onset (-5..+5) estimated separately by welfare arm; sequencing tests (inequality surge and public-service retrenchment LEAD LDI decline by 3-5 years in the thin arm only; no anticipatory welfare decline before erosion in the high arm). Ground onset in dossier A's episode list and dossier H's sequencing framework; guard reverse causality using the Szikra-Oektem (2023, JESP) finding that welfare states are transformed UNDER backsliding — quick search for that paper (<=10 min) or cite from its abstract.\n4. WELFARE-DEPENDENT DIVIDEND TEST: dossier B's ANRR event-study design restricted to post-1990 democratizers, split by first-decade welfare legacy; outcome: WID top-10% share (plus health+edu spending); then test whether first-decade dividend size predicts 2010-2022 LDI resilience.\n5. MECHANISM ARMS: (a) polarization: v2x_polarization as outcome of Gini x thin-welfare (mediation toward LDI decline) — anchored in dossier E's emphasis on polarization as proximate cause; (b) mismatch: Campante-Chor interaction template (dossier D) transposed: schooling/education expansion x ILO educated-youth-unemployment x thin welfare on Delta-LDI, with OWID availability from Phase 2.\nAlso produce: DIVERGENCES-TO-BRIDGE section — (i) Rau-Stokes uninterrupted-democracy sample 1995-2020 vs young democratizers 1989-2005: iter-2 must KEEP below-threshold democratizers with the continuous LDI outcome instead of censoring them; (ii) SOCX pension-heavy total spending vs the universal-services measure (health+edu) — the placebo arm; (iii) Houle's coup-era discrete survival vs graded erosion-era outcome; (iv) ANRR's 1960-2010 all-democratizations dividend vs the post-1990 wave; (v) H&K's case-based evidence vs the interaction design; and a MEASUREMENT-RISK section — PIP Gini 5-year waves/coverage in the young-democratizer sample, WHO GGHE-D pre-2000 gaps, WID interpolation, OWID vintage/anchor-year caveats — listing which items must go to follow_up_questions.\n\nPHASE 4 — WRITE OUTPUTS (2:50-3:00, 10 min)\nresearch_report.md sections: 1) Executive summary (the five specs + key calibration numbers, <=1 page); 2) Per-source extraction dossier (paper -> specs with quotes + URLs); 3) Mechanism->specification mapping table; 4) Divergences & bridging decisions; 5) OWID availability audit; 6) Measurement risks and open items; 7) Full source list with URLs/DOIs (mark PAYWALLED-ABSTRACT-ONLY where applicable).\nresearch_out.json: answer = <=800-word synthesis covering: what each study contributes, the five specs at specification level, the two must-bridge divergences, and the top-3 data risks; sources = every source actually used (title, url, doi, one-line contribution); follow_up_questions = actionable questions for iteration 2, including at minimum: (a) does OWID host V-Dem polarization and ILO educated-youth unemployment (and which proxy if not)?; (b) is Rau-Stokes replication data (episode list) public?; (c) PIP Gini coverage for post-1990 democratizers vs WID top-10 as treatment?; (d) WHO GGHE-D pre-2000 gaps — proxy with enrollment/immunization or later-start values?; (e) OECD SOCX composition confirmation for the placebo; (f) which regime classification (RoW vs BMR) defines the transition window?\nValidate research_out.json with a JSON parser if available (python -m json.tool via the tooling venv); otherwise manual review.\n\nPHASE 5 — QA (final minutes, fold into Phase 4)\n- No citation without a fetched source; no unexplained number; every number in the briefing has a URL+quote in notes.\n- Each of the five specifications is grounded in >=1 dossier.\n- Any item not extracted is explicitly marked UNVERIFIED in its dossier and mirrored into follow_up_questions.\n- Outputs exist, JSON is valid, and the report is self-contained enough for iteration 2's experiment designer to implement specs 1-5 without re-reading the primary papers.\n\nFAILURE SCENARIOS & FALLBACKS\n- Paywall (QJE, World Politics, ASR, CUP): NBER/author PDFs (Campante-Chor w16752, AES w17357, ANRR w19746, Houle's MSU page), Google Books preview for H&K, journal abstracts, and published reviews for case lists. Grep: \\\"hazard|survival|Weibull|Cox\\\", \\\"Gini\\\", \\\"lag|five-year|moving average\\\", \\\"control\\\", \\\"sample\\\", \\\"consolid\\\", \\\"breakdown\\\".\\nRecord: (a) outcome and sample (democracies, years, N regime breakdowns); (b) inequality operationalization (source: Deininger-Squire/WIID? interpolation? lagged moving average?); (c) model family (discrete-time? Cox? duration dependence?), controls, clustering; (d) key coefficients and interpretation for the young-democracy \\\"consolidation\\\" frame; (e) his operational definition of consolidation-as-survival (justifies the \\\"first democratic decade\\\" framing).\\n\\nD. Campante & Chor (2012, QJE) \u2014 the schooling x opportunities interaction template [20 min]\\nLocate NBER w16752 PDF (free) or Chor's faculty page; OUP QJE page for the final version. Grep: \\\"interaction\\\", \\\"schooling\\\", \\\"opportunit\\\", \\\"unemployment\\\", \\\"instrument\\\", \\\"probit|logit\\\", \\\"Arab\\\", \\\"coefficient\\\", \\\"Figure|Table\\\".\\nRecord: (a) unit of analysis (country-level and individual Arab Barometer); (b) DV coding (protest onset/intensity); (c) the two interacted regressors and their exact construction \u2014 schooling (average years / expansion) and the \\\"economic opportunities\\\" measure (components: educated-youth unemployment? income? corruption?); (d) the printed interaction equation and its sign pattern (schooling raises protest only where opportunities are poor); (e) endogeneity treatment (IV? lagged schooling growth?); (f) headline marginal effects \u2014 this calibrates the iter-2 mismatch arm (ILO educated-youth unemployment on OWID).\\n\\nE. Haggard & Kaufman (2021) \u2014 the coalitional null + case list [25 min]\\nLocate CUP book page + Google Books preview (search inside for the intro/ch.1 case summary); their Journal of Democracy pieces (\\\"Backsliding: Democratic Regress in the Contemporary World\\\", JD 2021; verify existence/title); published reviews (Perspectives on Politics, APSR) that summarize the case list; their 2016 Annual Review of Political Science \\\"Democratization During the Third Wave\\\" for welfare-state development in new democracies. Grep: \\\"backslider\\\", \\\"case\\\", \\\"Hungary\\\", \\\"Poland\\\", \\\"Turkey\\\", \\\"Venezuela\\\", \\\"South Africa\\\", \\\"inequality\\\", \\\"grievance\\\", \\\"polariz\\\", \\\"did not|no evidence\\\".\\nRecord: (a) their operational definition of backsliding (index, threshold, period); (b) the complete backslider list AND the survivors they compare against \u2014 critical for iter-2 sensitivity analysis (does the gate hold within H&K's own classification?); (c) the exact evidence for \\\"grievances/inequality did not differentiate\\\": inequality/crisis comparisons between backsliders and survivors (tables or reported numbers), and their polarization measures (V-Dem v2x_polarization? their own coding?); (d) their welfare-state material (2008 book \\\"Development, Democracy, and Welfare States\\\"; 2016 ARPS) on WHEN new democracies built welfare states \u2014 feeds the first-decade measurement design.\\n\\nF. Acemoglu, Egorov & Sonin (2013, QJE) \u2014 the pre-commitment mechanism [15 min]\\nLocate NBER w17357 PDF (free) or Acemoglu's MIT page. Grep: \\\"populist\\\", \\\"redistribut\\\", \\\"inequality\\\", \\\"Proposition\\\", \\\"equilibrium\\\", \\\"commit\\\".\\nRecord: (a) model skeleton: agents, candidates (established elite vs populist), timing, what the populist promises; (b) the \\\"endogenous limits to redistribution\\\" result \u2014 why high inequality makes elites limit redistribution and how that opens the populist equilibrium; (c) a plain-English mechanism statement usable in the paper (mechanism iii of the gate); (d) comparative statics that map to observable proxies (low pre-committed redistribution + high inequality => polarization/populist support).\\n\\nG. Policy feedback + universalism micro-evidence [20 min; trim to 10 if behind]\\nFetch abstracts/full texts as available: Pierson (1993, World Politics) \\\"When Effect Becomes Cause\\\" (policy feedback: programs create constituencies); Campbell (2012, Annual Review of Political Science) \\\"Policy Makes Mass Politics\\\"; Korpi & Palme (1998, ASR) \\\"The Paradox of Redistribution\\\" (universalism reduces inequality more than targeting \u2014 the result justifying a universal-services, not CCT, gate measure); Skocpol (1991) \\\"Targeting within Universalism\\\"; Manacorda, Miguel & Vigorito (2011, AEJ: Applied) PANES transfers -> incumbent support (cash transfers buy incumbents, not institutions); Zucco (2013) Bolsa Familia -> Lula vote; Dolls, Fuest & Peichl (2012, J. Public Economics) automatic stabilizers absorb ~38% (EU) vs ~32% (US) of income shocks (quantifies mechanism i); CEQ / \\\"Commitment to Equity\\\" (Lustig et al.) benefit-incidence evidence that health+education spending reaches lower/middle deciles in developing countries (mechanism ii); IMF Fiscal Monitor (Oct 2017) on fiscal redistribution and the middle class. Record per item: one-line takeaway + citation + which gate mechanism (i)/(ii)/(iii) or the Cash-vs-Services corollary it supports.\\n\\nH. Supporting regime-data papers [10 min]\\nLuehrmann-Tannenberg-Lindberg (2018, Democratization): RoW typology, what qualifies as entering \\\"electoral democracy\\\", transition-year conventions. Boix-Miller-Rosato (2013, CPS): democracy definition used as sample screen; note vintage/updates. V-Dem \\\"Backsliding in the World\\\" report only if instantly accessible (2020-2023 episode context). Record: definitional thresholds + the rule for coding \\\"post-1990 democratizer\\\" (transition 1989-2005) and \\\"uninterrupted democracy\\\".\\n\\nPHASE 2 \u2014 OWID AVAILABILITY AUDIT (2:15-2:30, 15 min)\\nVerify by fetching each OWID page (pages only, NO dataset downloads): series exists, its source attribution, date coverage; record slug + caveats in notes/08_owid_audit.md:\\n(a) liberal-democracy-index (V-Dem v2x_libdem); (b) electoral-democracy-index and Regimes-of-the-World categories if hosted; (c) World Bank PIP Gini page and the WID Gini page; (d) WID top-10% share page; (e) domestic-health-expenditure-share-gdp (WHO GGHE-D) \u2014 CRITICAL: scrutinize start years (many countries have no pre-2000 values; first-decade end for 1989-95 transitions is 1999-2005); (f) government-expenditure-on-education-share-of-gdp (WB EdStats); (g) total-social-expenditure-gdp-oecd (OECD SOCX) \u2014 record any metadata on composition (pensions share) for the placebo arm; (h) political-polarization page (V-Dem v2x_polarization) \u2014 hosted?; (i) ILO education/mismatch series (unemployment by education level, especially educated youth) \u2014 hosted? If (h)/(i) are absent, that goes into follow_up_questions with OWID proxy candidates (overall youth unemployment, school enrollment).\\n\\nPHASE 3 \u2014 SYNTHESIS: THE DESIGN BRIEFING (2:30-2:50, 20 min)\\nDistill everything into the mechanism->specification mapping iteration 2 will implement. The report MUST specify, for each of the five specifications, the concrete form \u2014 variables, lags, fixed effects, clustering, sample, controls \u2014 and cite the source(s) each element comes from:\\n1. GATE INTERACTION (core): e.g., LDI_{c,t} ~ Gini_{c,t-5} + Welf_c0 + (Gini x Welf_c0) + controls, country+year FE, cluster by country; Welf_c0 = health+edu spending %GDP at first-decade end, time-invariant (pre-determined). Theory expectation: inequality effect ~0 in the high-welfare arm, strongly negative in the thin arm (use dossier B's dividend logic + dossier F's commitment logic as the theoretical underpinning; dossier G for the universalism justification).\\n2. ADJUDICATION SPEC: same sample, first WITHOUT interaction (baseline main effect \u2014 compare sign/magnitude to dossier A's Rau-Stokes estimate), then WITH interaction. This is the empirical reconciliation of Rau-Stokes (non-zero main effect) vs Haggard-Kaufman (no systematic economic grievance): the effect is concentrated in the thin-welfare arm.\\n3. EROSION-ONSET EVENT STUDIES + LEAD-LAG SEQUENCING: dynamic coefficients around erosion onset (-5..+5) estimated separately by welfare arm; sequencing tests (inequality surge and public-service retrenchment LEAD LDI decline by 3-5 years in the thin arm only; no anticipatory welfare decline before erosion in the high arm). Ground onset in dossier A's episode list and dossier H's sequencing framework; guard reverse causality using the Szikra-Oektem (2023, JESP) finding that welfare states are transformed UNDER backsliding \u2014 quick search for that paper (<=10 min) or cite from its abstract.\\n4. WELFARE-DEPENDENT DIVIDEND TEST: dossier B's ANRR event-study design restricted to post-1990 democratizers, split by first-decade welfare legacy; outcome: WID top-10% share (plus health+edu spending); then test whether first-decade dividend size predicts 2010-2022 LDI resilience.\\n5. MECHANISM ARMS: (a) polarization: v2x_polarization as outcome of Gini x thin-welfare (mediation toward LDI decline) \u2014 anchored in dossier E's emphasis on polarization as proximate cause; (b) mismatch: Campante-Chor interaction template (dossier D) transposed: schooling/education expansion x ILO educated-youth-unemployment x thin welfare on Delta-LDI, with OWID availability from Phase 2.\\nAlso produce: DIVERGENCES-TO-BRIDGE section \u2014 (i) Rau-Stokes uninterrupted-democracy sample 1995-2020 vs young democratizers 1989-2005: iter-2 must KEEP below-threshold democratizers with the continuous LDI outcome instead of censoring them; (ii) SOCX pension-heavy total spending vs the universal-services measure (health+edu) \u2014 the placebo arm; (iii) Houle's coup-era discrete survival vs graded erosion-era outcome; (iv) ANRR's 1960-2010 all-democratizations dividend vs the post-1990 wave; (v) H&K's case-based evidence vs the interaction design; and a MEASUREMENT-RISK section \u2014 PIP Gini 5-year waves/coverage in the young-democratizer sample, WHO GGHE-D pre-2000 gaps, WID interpolation, OWID vintage/anchor-year caveats \u2014 listing which items must go to follow_up_questions.\\n\\nPHASE 4 \u2014 WRITE OUTPUTS (2:50-3:00, 10 min)\\nresearch_report.md sections: 1) Executive summary (the five specs + key calibration numbers, <=1 page); 2) Per-source extraction dossier (paper -> specs with quotes + URLs); 3) Mechanism->specification mapping table; 4) Divergences & bridging decisions; 5) OWID availability audit; 6) Measurement risks and open items; 7) Full source list with URLs/DOIs (mark PAYWALLED-ABSTRACT-ONLY where applicable).\\nresearch_out.json: answer = <=800-word synthesis covering: what each study contributes, the five specs at specification level, the two must-bridge divergences, and the top-3 data risks; sources = every source actually used (title, url, doi, one-line contribution); follow_up_questions = actionable questions for iteration 2, including at minimum: (a) does OWID host V-Dem polarization and ILO educated-youth unemployment (and which proxy if not)?; (b) is Rau-Stokes replication data (episode list) public?; (c) PIP Gini coverage for post-1990 democratizers vs WID top-10 as treatment?; (d) WHO GGHE-D pre-2000 gaps \u2014 proxy with enrollment/immunization or later-start values?; (e) OECD SOCX composition confirmation for the placebo; (f) which regime classification (RoW vs BMR) defines the transition window?\\nValidate research_out.json with a JSON parser if available (python -m json.tool via the tooling venv); otherwise manual review.\\n\\nPHASE 5 \u2014 QA (final minutes, fold into Phase 4)\\n- No citation without a fetched source; no unexplained number; every number in the briefing has a URL+quote in notes.\\n- Each of the five specifications is grounded in >=1 dossier.\\n- Any item not extracted is explicitly marked UNVERIFIED in its dossier and mirrored into follow_up_questions.\\n- Outputs exist, JSON is valid, and the report is self-contained enough for iteration 2's experiment designer to implement specs 1-5 without re-reading the primary papers.\\n\\nFAILURE SCENARIOS & FALLBACKS\\n- Paywall (QJE, World Politics, ASR, CUP): NBER/author PDFs (Campante-Chor w16752, AES w17357, ANRR w19746, Houle's MSU page), Google Books preview for H&K, journal abstracts, and published reviews for case lists. Mark PAYWALLED-ABSTRACT-ONLY; extract what is available; never fabricate.\\n- PNAS SI inaccessible: work from the main text; note which tables came from the SI.\\n- Search engine failure / rate limits: switch to --mode scholarly (OpenAlex/Crossref); retry with backoff; if the ability server is down, report it rather than improvising.\\n- Time pressure: prioritize A (Rau-Stokes), B (ANRR dividend), E (H&K case list), D (Campante-Chor), then the OWID audit; trim G/H to abstracts; compress F to the mechanism statement.\\n- Conflicting metadata (e.g., ANRR venue): record both with URLs and flag for the experiment (the citation matters for the paper's references).\\n\\nCONSTRAINTS\\n- Zero LLM spend: this artifact needs NO OpenRouter calls (the $10 budget is untouched); all work uses free web tooling.\\n- All files written inside the executor's workspace (absolute paths, subdirectories notes/ OK).\\n- No dataset downloads, no data computation, no model estimation \u2014 this is pure information gathering + synthesis. The only scripts used are the web-tool search/fetch/grep helpers.\",",
  "new_str": "\"research_plan\": \"RESEARCH EXECUTOR ROLE || DELIVERABLES: (1) research_report.md - the DESIGN BRIEFING (main deliverable, sections per STEP 4); (2) research_out.json with answer, sources, follow_up_questions; (3) per-source notes under notes/ (00_sources.md, 01_rau_stokes.md, 02_anrr.md, 03_houle.md, 04_campante_chor.md, 05_haggard_kaufman.md, 06_aes_populism.md, 07_policy_feedback.md, 08_owid_audit.md, 09_datasets.md) so nothing is lost on timeout. || TOOLING: read the aii-web-tools skill first; prefer built-in WebSearch/WebFetch (load once via ToolSearch select:WebSearch,WebFetch if deferred); otherwise use aii_fast_web_search.py --query <q> --mode scholarly (OpenAlex/Crossref for DOIs) or --mode general (discovery), and aii_fast_web_fetch.py fetch --url <u> --max-chars N and grep --url <u> --pattern <regex> -i for exact text and numbers in long pages and PDFs. Workflow per source: search (scholarly) -> fetch (gist) -> grep (exact equations, numbers, case lists); grep PDFs with 200-300 char context; parallelize aggressively (4-6 independent searches or fetches per turn) and sequence only fetch-after-search; if the ability server is down, say so in the report and do not improvise another search method. VERIFICATION RULES: every number, case, equation and quote must trace to a fetched source (URL plus snippet in notes); never guess citations or DOIs - get them from Crossref/OpenAlex or the publisher page; if a source is paywalled, mark it PAYWALLED-ABSTRACT-ONLY, extract only what the abstract/Google Books/review gives, never invent details, and move missing items to follow_up_questions. || STEP 0 - SOURCE LOCK (0:00-0:15): run one scholarly-mode search per target in parallel: (1) Rau Stokes income inequality erosion democracy PNAS; (2) Acemoglu Naidu Restrepo Robinson democracy redistribution inequality; (3) Houle inequality and democracy why inequality harms consolidation but not democratization; (4) Campante Chor why was the Arab world poised for revolution schooling economic opportunities; (5) Acemoglu Egorov Sonin a political theory of populism; (6) Haggard Kaufman backsliding democratic regress in the contemporary world; (7) Korpi Palme paradox of redistribution strategies of equality; (8) Pierson when effect becomes cause policy feedback and political change. Record in 00_sources.md a status column (FOUND, FETCHED, EXTRACTED, PAYWALLED-ABSTRACT-ONLY) plus one fetchable URL per item: PNAS article page and its Supplementary PDF (pnas.*.sapp.pdf); NBER working-paper PDFs; author-hosted PDFs (Houle MSU faculty page, Chor faculty page, Acemoglu MIT page); publisher landing pages (OUP QJE, Cambridge World Politics, Sage JESP, Annual Reviews); Google Books preview for the Cambridge book. Also confirm two regime-data papers: Luehrmann-Tannenberg-Lindberg Regimes of the World (Democratization 2018) and Boix-Miller-Rosato A Complete Data Set of Political Regimes 1800-2007 (CPS 2013) - they define transition dates and the uninterrupted-democracy sample the experiment inherits. NOTE: the hypothesis cites ANRR 2015 as AER but the expected reality is a Handbook of Income Distribution Vol.2 (Elsevier 2015) chapter with NBER w19746 as the working paper; VERIFY the true venue via Crossref/OpenAlex and record both citations. || STEP 1 - EXTRACTION DOSSIERS A-H (0:15-2:15): for each: fetch main text, grep exact items, record into the numbered notes file. Priority when time is short: A, B, E, D are load-bearing; trim G/H first; F can compress to a mechanism statement. [A] Rau and Stokes 2025 PNAS (25 min): locate via PNAS DOI plus SI PDF; grep patterns: uninterrupted, sample, erosion, episode, Gini|SWIID|PIP|inequality, hazard|Cox|logit|OLS|probit, control, cluster, lag, 1995|2000|2020, threshold; record: (a) exact sample definition - which regime classification screens countries, which countries are excluded (post-1990 democratizers that fell below the democracy threshold?), N countries, N country-years, period; (b) the full erosion-episode list and coding rule (binary onset? sustained V-Dem LDI drop? threshold?); (c) inequality variable: exact source (SWIID/PIP/WID), transformation, lag structure; (d) full control set; (e) model family, fixed effects, clustering; (f) headline coefficients and SEs (calibrates the iter-2 main-effect magnitude); (g) whether they run ANY moderation or interaction analysis (expected none - that gap is the gate); (h) replication-data availability; spend at most 5 min on published commentary or replication critiques. [B] ANRR 2015 dividend design (30 min): NBER w19746 PDF plus published-version landing page; grep: democratiz, event stud, top decile|top 10|income share, school|education, tax, public good, matching, 1960, equation, cluster, sample; record: (a) democratization-event definition and regime-classification source; (b) sample N countries and years; (c) the full outcome list - top-decile income share (WID?), tax/GDP share, social/education/health spending, schooling; (d) estimation design - OLS with country and year FE, dynamic leads/lags event-study form, any matching estimator; write out their printed equation(s); (e) controls and clustering; (f) headline magnitudes: effect of democratization on the top-decile share and public-goods outcomes at +10 years (sizes the iter-2 welfare-dependent dividend test); (g) their own caveats on the inequality leg (the exact leg the hypothesis claims is welfare-gated); skim ANRR 2019 JPE Democracy Does Cause Growth (at most 10 min) only for event-study window and timing conventions. [C] Houle 2009 World Politics (15 min): Cambridge Core landing page plus full-text PDF (MSU faculty page; Project MUSE as fallback); grep: hazard|survival|Weibull|Cox, Gini, lag|five-year|moving average, control, sample, consolid, breakdown; record: (a) outcome and sample (democracies, years, N regime breakdowns); (b) inequality operationalization (source, interpolation, lagged moving average); (c) model family, controls, clustering; (d) key coefficients for the young-democracy consolidation frame; (e) his operational definition of consolidation-as-survival (justifies the first-democratic-decade framing). [D] Campante and Chor 2012 QJE (20 min): NBER w16752 PDF or Chor faculty page; grep: interaction, schooling, opportunit, unemployment, instrument, probit|logit, Arab, coefficient, Figure|Table; record: (a) unit of analysis (country-level and individual Arab Barometer); (b) DV coding (protest onset or intensity); (c) the two interacted regressors and their exact construction - schooling and the economic-opportunities measure with its components; (d) the printed interaction equation and its sign pattern (schooling raises protest only where opportunities are poor); (e) endogeneity treatment (IV? lagged schooling growth?); (f) headline marginal effects - calibrates the iter-2 mismatch arm (ILO educated-youth unemployment). [E] Haggard and Kaufman 2021 (25 min): Cambridge book page plus Google Books preview, their Journal of Democracy pieces, published reviews (Perspectives on Politics, APSR) that summarize the case list, and their 2016 Annual Review of Political Science Democratization During the Third Wave; grep: backslider, case, Hungary, Poland, Turkey, Venezuela, South Africa, inequality, grievance, polariz, did not|no evidence; record: (a) their operational definition of backsliding (index, threshold, period); (b) the complete backslider list AND the survivor comparison set - critical for iter-2 sensitivity analysis (does the gate hold within their own classification?); (c) the exact evidence for the claim that grievances and inequality did not differentiate outcomes (tables or reported numbers) and their polarization measures (V-Dem v2x_polarization or their own coding); (d) their welfare-state material (2008 book Development Democracy and Welfare States; 2016 ARPS) on WHEN new democracies built welfare states - feeds the first-decade measurement design. [F] Acemoglu Egorov Sonin 2013 QJE (15 min): NBER w17357 PDF or Acemoglu MIT page; grep: populist, redistribut, inequality, Proposition, equilibrium, commit; record: (a) model skeleton (agents, candidates, timing, what the populist promises); (b) the endogenous-limits-to-redistribution result - why high inequality makes elites limit redistribution and opens the populist equilibrium; (c) a plain-English mechanism statement for mechanism (iii) pre-commitment of the gate; (d) comparative statics mappable to observable proxies (low pre-committed redistribution plus high inequality implies polarization and populist support). [G] Policy feedback and universalism micro-evidence (20 min, trim to 10 if behind): fetch abstracts or full texts as available: Pierson 1993 World Politics When Effect Becomes Cause (programs create constituencies); Campbell 2012 Annual Review of Political Science Policy Makes Mass Politics; Korpi and Palme 1998 ASR The Paradox of Redistribution (universalism reduces inequality more than targeting - justifies a universal-services not a cash-transfer gate measure); Skocpol 1991 Targeting within Universalism; Manacorda Miguel Vigorito 2011 AEJ Applied PANES transfers and incumbent support (cash buys incumbents, not institutions); Zucco 2013 Bolsa Familia and Lula vote; Dolls Fuest Peichl 2012 J Public Economics automatic stabilizers absorb roughly 38 percent of income shocks in the EU and 32 percent in the US (quantifies mechanism i); CEQ Commitment to Equity benefit-incidence evidence that health and education spending reaches lower and middle deciles of the distribution in developing countries (mechanism ii); IMF Fiscal Monitor October 2017 on fiscal redistribution and the middle class. Record per item: one-line takeaway, citation, and which gate mechanism (i) automatic stabilization, (ii) constituency formation, (iii) pre-commitment or the cash-versus-services corollary it supports. [H] Regime-data papers (10 min): RoW 2018 - typology, what qualifies as entering electoral democracy, transition-year conventions; BMR 2013 - democracy definition used as the sample screen, data vintage; V-Dem Backsliding in the World report only if instantly accessible. Record definitional thresholds plus the rule for coding a post-1990 democratizer (transition 1989-2005) and an uninterrupted democracy. || STEP 2 - OWID AVAILABILITY AUDIT (2:15-2:30, 15 min): verify by fetching each OWID page (pages only - NO dataset downloads); record the series slug, source attribution, date coverage, and caveats in 08_owid_audit.md: (a) liberal-democracy-index (V-Dem v2x_libdem); (b) electoral-democracy-index and Regimes of the World categories if hosted; (c) World Bank PIP Gini page and the WID Gini page; (d) WID top-10-percent income-share page; (e) domestic-health-expenditure-share-of-gdp (WHO GGHE-D) - CRITICAL: scrutinize start years; many countries have no pre-2000 values, and the first-decade end for 1989-95 transitions is 1999-2005; (f) government-expenditure-on-education-share-of-gdp (WB EdStats); (g) total-social-expenditure-gdp-oecd (OECD SOCX) - record any metadata on composition (pensions share) for the placebo arm; (h) political-polarization (V-Dem v2x_polarization) - hosted?; (i) ILO education and mismatch series (unemployment by education level, educated youth) - hosted? If (h) or (i) is absent, move it to follow_up_questions with OWID proxy candidates (overall youth unemployment, school enrollment). || STEP 3 - SYNTHESIS: THE DESIGN BRIEFING (2:30-2:50, 20 min): distill everything into the mechanism-to-specification mapping. The report MUST give, for each of the five specifications, the concrete form (variables, lags, fixed effects, clustering, sample, controls) and cite which dossier each element comes from: (1) GATE INTERACTION (core): LDI_{c,t} ~ Gini_{c,t-5} + Welf_c0 + (Gini x Welf_c0) + controls, country and year fixed effects, cluster by country; Welf_c0 = health plus education spending as percent of GDP measured at the end of the first democratic decade, time-invariant and pre-determined; expectation: the inequality effect is near zero in the high-welfare arm and strongly negative in the thin arm (dossier B dividend logic plus dossier F commitment logic as theory, dossier G universalism as justification). (2) ADJUDICATION SPEC: same sample, first WITHOUT the interaction (baseline main effect - compare sign and magnitude with dossier A Rau-Stokes estimate), then WITH the interaction; this is the empirical reconciliation of the two literatures (effect concentrated in the thin-welfare arm). (3) EROSION-ONSET EVENT STUDIES AND LEAD-LAG SEQUENCING: dynamic coefficients around erosion onset (-5 to +5) estimated separately by welfare arm; sequencing tests - inequality surges and public-service retrenchment LEAD LDI declines by 3-5 years in the thin arm only, and NO anticipatory welfare decline before erosion onset in the high arm; ground the onset coding in dossier A episode list and dossier H sequencing; guard reverse causality with Szikra and Oektem 2023 JESP (welfare states are transformed UNDER backsliding; quick search at most 10 min or cite from abstract). (4) WELFARE-DEPENDENT DIVIDEND TEST: dossier B ANRR event-study design restricted to post-1990 democratizers, split by first-decade welfare legacy; outcome: WID top-10 percent share (plus health and education spending); then test whether first-decade dividend size predicts 2010-2022 LDI resilience. (5) MECHANISM ARMS: (a) polarization - v2x_polarization as outcome of Gini x thin-welfare (mediation toward LDI decline), anchored in dossier E; (b) mismatch - Campante-Chor interaction template (dossier D) transposed: schooling and education expansion x ILO educated-youth unemployment x thin welfare on Delta-LDI, with OWID availability from STEP 2. Also produce a DIVERGENCES-TO-BRIDGE section: (i) Rau-Stokes uninterrupted-democracy sample 1995-2020 versus young democratizers 1989-2005 - iter-2 must KEEP below-threshold democratizers with the continuous LDI outcome instead of censoring them; (ii) SOCX pension-heavy total social spending versus the universal-services measure (health plus education) - the placebo arm; (iii) Houle coup-era discrete survival versus the graded erosion-era outcome; (iv) ANRR 1960-2010 all-democratizations dividend versus the post-1990 wave; (v) Haggard-Kaufman case-based evidence versus the interaction design. Plus a MEASUREMENT-RISK section: PIP Gini 5-year waves and coverage in the young-democratizer sample, WHO GGHE-D pre-2000 gaps, WID interpolation, OWID vintage and anchor-year caveats - listing which items must go to follow_up_questions. || STEP 4 - WRITE OUTPUTS (2:50-3:00, 10 min): research_report.md sections: 1) Executive summary (the five specs and key calibration numbers, at most one page); 2) Per-source extraction dossier with quotes and URLs; 3) Mechanism-to-specification mapping table; 4) Divergences and bridging decisions; 5) OWID availability audit; 6) Measurement risks and open items; 7) Full source list with URLs and DOIs (mark PAYWALLED-ABSTRACT-ONLY where applicable). research_out.json: answer at most 800 words covering what each study contributes, the five specs at specification level, the two must-bridge divergences, and the top-3 data risks; sources - every source actually used (title, url, doi, one-line contribution); follow_up_questions - at minimum: (a) does OWID host V-Dem polarization and ILO educated-youth unemployment (and which proxy if not)?; (b) is the Rau-Stokes replication data (episode list) public?; (c) PIP Gini coverage for post-1990 democratizers versus WID top-10 as the treatment?; (d) WHO GGHE-D pre-2000 gaps - proxy with enrollment and immunization or use later-start values?; (e) OECD SOCX composition confirmation for the placebo; (f) which regime classification (RoW versus BMR) defines the transition window? Validate research_out.json with a JSON parser if available (python -m json.tool via the tooling venv); otherwise manual review. || STEP 5 - QA (fold into STEP 4): no citation without a fetched source; no unexplained number - every number in the briefing has a URL plus quote in notes; each of the five specifications grounded in at least one dossier; any item not extracted is explicitly marked UNVERIFIED in its dossier and mirrored into follow_up_questions; outputs exist, JSON is valid, and the report is self-contained enough that iteration 2 implements specs 1-5 without re-reading the primary papers. || FAILURE SCENARIOS AND FALLBACKS: paywall (QJE, World Politics, ASR, Cambridge) - use NBER or author PDFs (w16752, w17357, w19746, Houle MSU page), Google Books preview for Haggard-Kaufman, journal abstracts, and published reviews for case lists; mark PAYWALLED-ABSTRACT-ONLY and never fabricate.
